built the continuation context and asked for a Turn only to hear `lane_busy`
again: the knowledge lead logged 2,555 such refusals and then 3,176 deferred
`turn_persist` cycles (about 50 a minute for an hour) while one Turn ran.
Decision: the refusal names the busy Turn (`LifecycleConflict.turn`), and the
service records it as the lane's hold (`continuation.hold_lane`, process-local,
one entry per Coordinator conversation). While the held attempt is pending or
running, a pass skips that lane's requests after one attempt read per pass,
before deriving anything. The Turn's own settlement drain
(`turn_lifecycle._drain_queue_after_settlement`) is the event that runs the pass
which births the wake, exactly once. The hold is a hint: an ended or unreadable
holder drops it and the ordinary birth attempt arbitrates the lane, so a holder
that settled in another process delays nothing. No interval, count or cap was
added. Alternatives rejected: a debounce on delivery-lane wakes (a tuned
interval that still repeats); a durable queue of deferred births (the
authorized row already is the durable obligation).
Specification: `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_wake_lane_events.py`.

#### A deferred wake starts with its brief rebuilt at birth (2026-11-18)

Problem: wake 7a04ce76 of the tofu lead was authorized at 00:01, refused
`lane_busy` until 08:34 and born (Turn 371ea08a) with the brief stored at
authorization, whose instruction a 04:36 change had replaced; a commitment
wake would equally announce promises kept meanwhile as due. Decision: the
stored `brief` stays the authorization record (CAS equality, audit). At birth
`continuation.wake_brief` keeps what the wake was authorized for (a restart
note, an applied decision default), renders the instruction from the current
`continuation_instruction`, and lists only the wake's commitments the current
board still holds open at the same due time (none left: a plain continue).
The appended continuation context was already read at birth. The owed wake
itself still fires (`test_a_seen_result_still_wakes_when_nothing_else_will`).
Alternatives rejected: revoking and re-authorizing a stale wake (a revoked
last wake ends the request's continuation); holding back an old wake when no
Worker needs the lead (it also suppresses the wake a request needs to report
and finish). Specification:
`test_a_long_deferred_wake_starts_with_its_brief_rebuilt_from_current_state`.

#### A request whose authority ended is retired once (owner request 2026-11-18)

Problem: a request whose Coordinator was stopped, rebound, or whose human source
changed stayed open forever. Every pass re-derived the same "Original
Coordinator request was stopped" verdict for it (three tofu requests, every
lane event since 11-18 00:01). Decision: the authority checks of
`continuation._proposal` raise `RequestAuthorityEnded` for a non-transient
verdict, and recovery records the request's one `coordinatorSettlement` with
reason `authority_ended` (no `reportTurnId`: there is no report). The
settlement CAS derives the verdict again from its own snapshot, so a request
whose authority is live is never closed. Discovery excludes settled requests,
so the verdict is never derived again. Alternatives rejected: a process-local
verdict memo (every process and restart re-derives it); revoking the wake grant
(revocation is a Stop's own record, not a verdict about the request).

#### A result the last Turn saw starts no Turn (owner request 2026-11-18)

Problem: 4 of 9 idle-wait Turn ends were followed within 4 s by a wake that only
polled ($4.84): a wake queued while the lane was busy, or derived from the
ending Turn's own reviews, started after that Turn had been told the results it
was for. Decision: when a Coordinator attempt completes, its settlement drain
records what its polls reported (`coordinator_workers._REPORTS_KEY`: each
Worker's settlement at attempt and delivery receipt) as the lane's observation
(`continuation.observe_turn_end`). A results wake, newly derived or queued and
unborn, starts no Turn while every Worker of its request that needs the lead
(needs review, held, interrupted, ended) was reported at its current settlement
and another Worker of the request is still running or delivering. That
Worker's settlement is new evidence and wakes the lead; a queued wake is kept,
not revoked, and is born then. Without a pending Worker, or for a commitment or
decision wake, the wake starts as before. Project-message wakes already name
the newest pending message, and a Turn that read its messages leaves none
pending ("Messages wake an idle lead"). Alternatives rejected: time windows
around the Turn end (a tuned interval); dropping queued wakes (loses the
request's continuation). Specification:
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_wake_lane_events.py`.

#### Failed results wakes are not idle waits

A failed results Turn did not finish handling its Worker frontier. When the
frontier still has an actionable result, a running or held sibling Worker and
an older completed Turn's observation do not suppress the existing stall
backoff. A later successful Turn on the same lane can discharge that wake
while a sibling is pending, if its observation covers the current frontier.
Settlement and startup rederive the due time from the failed Attempt. An
existing unborn wake keeps its command and grant, but cannot bind after its
pinned predecessor grant is revoked or its execution association advances.
The old Turn is never regenerated. On unchanged evidence, a manual retry
verdict commits the blocked decision and report intent before publication;
startup replays an unacknowledged report through the same report command.
Only an affirmative worker-exit classification (`cutCallsClassifiedSafe`)
permits a fresh wake for the no-cut-call exception, not replay of the failed
Attempt. Fresh Worker evidence and due decisions or commitments retain their
existing paths. An unresolved future owner decision keeps the request open
even when the results retry must stop, including when a later Turn has already
seen the queued retry's results. Successful Turns retain the exact Worker
result revision and delivery status returned by a poll in their existing
settlement. Results-only admission rechecks that evidence and fences the
Coordinator conversation revision at Turn creation, so a concurrent settlement
between the read and commit requires a fresh recovery decision. Stop receipts
also fence the selected Worker dispatch documents, whose point updates do not
advance the project head. Worker and Coordinator Turn revisions, current
Attempts and verdicts are checked in that same dispatch transaction: a Worker
can settle without changing its assignment. The SQL authority holds the owned
Turn rows through commit; the native authority validates its point reads with
its existing transaction witness. A mismatch records neither stop nor report.
These are distinct fences: a Turn pair can be accepted
before its dispatch association. A definitive bind refusal settles only an
unbound Attempt in the terminal-event transaction, without a separate start
claim. If that write does not commit, ordinary accepted-attempt recovery still
sees the same pair; if it does commit, replay sees the terminal outcome. A
legacy start marker is not safe to adopt during serving. The existing
retired-generation Turn sweep settles it after the normal liveness fence. Exhaustion and
manual-retry verdicts do not rewrite the task board or the Workers' grants,
checkpoints, or holds. Alternatives rejected: waiting for a held Worker to
settle, replaying the failed Turn, or adding another wake timer.
Specification: `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_continuation.py`.

#### Lead prompts carry no ceremony (owner request 2026-11-18)

Problem: the 2026-11-18 lead audit (D1, D2, D4, T4, T5) found lead prompts at a
median 124k–417k input tokens per request, filled with reads the prompts
themselves asked for. Decision, each removing a read or call rather than
capping one:

- Delivery accepts project messages ("Passive project conversation messages"):
  `consider_project_message` remains for rejections, deferrals and conflicts.
- "First check get_project_workers" appears only on a Turn whose input is the
  owner's own message (`project_coordination.OWNER_TURN_GUIDANCE`); a wake or
  relay already carries the states it acts on. The same role text tells a lead
  that a poll's settlement carries the whole final answer, and the Global
  Router which owner-message Turn its request is (ROUTER.md R2).
- Relay notices and continuation briefs no longer send the lead back to
  `get_conversation`/`get_project_assignment` (ROUTER.md R3).

Measured on the four leads' 58 h transcripts (visible result characters):

| Removed per 58 h | Before |
|---|---|
| `consider_project_message` calls that only accepted (321 of 321 decisions but 1 deferral) | 321 calls, 621.8k chars |
| `Message revision changed` refusals | 19 |
| `get_project_workers` in non-owner Turns | 18 calls, 310.3k chars |
| `get_conversation` re-reads in relay Turns (Global Router) | 24 calls, 120.8k chars |
| `list_conversations` searches for the owner's message | GR 11 (1,791 s, 4 timeouts), TL 5 (305 s) |

About 1.05M result characters stop entering lead contexts, each also re-sent
with every later request until compaction. The role tool schemas are not
trimmed: every tool a lead never called in the 58 h is a capability the owner
granted on purpose (R9–R13, and since 2026-11-18 `owner_delegation` and
`set_model` reach every lead), the Claude route's name budget already hides the
least used ones, and the projection of a Project Router's `control_conversation`
without the Router-only part was withdrawn when that part became every lead's.
Alternatives rejected: shrinking descriptions or result budgets (truncation
sizes are knobs, AGENTS.md principle 5); removing unused tools from the
ceilings (`todo_write` writes the same board by owner decision, "One progress
source"); taking `consider_project_message` out of
the Project Coordinator's direct core (measured: the name-budget fit then
admits `await_device` and `create_memory`, 48.6k → 50.7k wire characters).
Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_coordination.py::test_only_an_owner_turn_rereads_workers_and_the_router_sees_its_request_turn`,
`tests/router/test_question_relay.py`.

#### Settlement is news, reported once (owner report 2026-11-15)

Problem: the owner saw finished Workers whose next step waited on unrelated
ones. The Coordinator `coordinator-4950501b927a581b666a974cb9ce21b5` polled
10–16 IDs with `wait_s=1500`; its transcript and `logs/app.log` on 2026-11-15
give, per Worker, settlement → poll return → review → canonical delivery. 21
settlements landed while a poll naming the Worker was open, which returned within
the logs' 1–3 s resolution (wake latency is not a cause). The 12 that landed
while the Coordinator
was busy (generating, reviewing another Worker, or in a call) waited for the
next *unrelated* settlement, because a poll ignored every Worker already
settled at call start: c9825ced 1,120 s (16:23:59 → 16:42:40), 6e3350d8 355 s
then 3,715 s to review, 111d9a5e 354 s, eec5f732 313 s, 4596a2bd 262 s,
4ea26e1e 346 s, c95ff265 117 s, 6e3350d8 again 105 s, 913bd28e 84 s, 82fee7a1
69 s, 54e1f09a 60 s, 20f51a47 24 s. That rule was the dominant delay. Second: each poll
repeated every settled, reviewed and delivered Worker (b0beae29, 7a9666f6), and
an accepted delivery counted as pending, so news hid in noise and review waited
behind reading (c93f283c: 2,119 s from settlement to review; 611cdd2b 178 s,
a6142b62 308 s). Third, independent of the Coordinator: review to canonical
delivery took 11–400 s in the publisher (50ea23eb 400 s, 913bd28e 240 s,
1a7ab074 236 s), the receiver lanes' own latency.

Decision: a settlement is an event, identified by (phase, attempt task,
delivery receipt status), and each one is reported once per Coordinator
attempt: the attempt's task holds the identity it last reported per Worker.
News ends a wait at once whenever it happened, and any Worker of the request
ends it, named or not, so nothing settled waits behind a pending Worker and a
settlement wakes a poll blocked on other IDs. Execution and publication are
separate pending phases; review-needed, held and delivered are separate settled
phases. A poll reads results only for its news. Publication of an accepted
result already starts at the review itself (`review_for_coordinator` wakes the
receiver), so the Coordinator's next turn is never on the publication path; its
held or canonical receipt is reported once, like any settlement. What the
Coordinator still serializes is its own judgment: reviews, rework and follow-up
delegations are model decisions in one context, taken in the order news
arrives; since a poll returns each settlement as it lands and never holds one
back, one Worker's review waits only for the decisions the Coordinator is
already making, never for another Worker to finish.

Alternatives rejected: per-Worker settlement turns (a concurrent Coordinator
Turn per settlement) would split one plan across contexts that each decide
shared work (merge order, follow-ups that depend on several results) without
seeing the others, and two reviews could race on one checkout's publication;
the serial cost measured above came from the poll rule, not from the
Coordinator handling one result at a time. A durable reported-set per Worker
would survive a new attempt, but a new attempt is a new model context that
must hear the settlements again (its wake brief lists them). A debounce or
batch window to collect several settlements per return is a tuned interval
(AGENTS.md principle 5) and delays the first one. Keeping running rows in
`workers` repeated pending Workers in every result; their IDs suffice.

Restarts (owner follow-up 2026-11-15): a call a restart cut off is classified
by its declared effect at boot ([Run continuity](RUN_CONTINUITY.md), "A
restart-cut call is classified by its declared effect"). A Worker whose cut
calls are all read-only, idempotent or verifiable resumes from its checkpoint
by itself and reaches the poll as phase `resuming` (nothing to do). Only a call
of unknown effect class (a push, an install, a write outside the Worker's
worktree) makes the interruption a settlement with phase `needs_decision`,
reported once like any other with `error.unknownEffects`; the Coordinator checks
the named calls and resumes, stops or follows up the Worker through
`control_conversation` from any of its Turns ("Session control is scoped by the
Coordinator binding" below). The phase is part of the settlement identity, as
is the attempt, so a Worker resumed and interrupted again is news again.

Parallel polls (2026-11-15): two `poll` calls of one tool batch share the
attempt's reported-settlement record. Problem: both could read the same
settlement as news and both report it, or one could overwrite the other's
claim. Decision: the record carries a lock; a poll computes its news and claims
it in one critical section, then reads results outside it, and puts its claims
back if that read fails, so each settlement is reported by exactly one poll.
The one seeding read of the request's pending Workers runs under the same lock.
Alternatives rejected: a compare-and-swap retry loop over an immutable record
(the same exclusion with a retry path and more code) and serializing whole
polls (a poll blocked for up to `wait_s` would block the other).
Specification: `test_parallel_polls_of_one_batch_report_each_settlement_once`.

Verify `test_a_settlement_wakes_a_wait_on_other_workers_and_waits_behind_none`,
`test_a_restart_cut_call_of_unknown_effect_is_a_needs_decision_settlement_reported_once`,
`test_a_settlement_reported_once_never_ends_a_later_wait_early` and
`test_a_coordinators_own_review_is_no_news_and_a_delivered_worker_stops_reappearing`
in `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_workers.py`.

#### A lead reads each result once (owner request 2026-11-18)

Problem: the lead's own context, not its Workers, was the costliest part of a
request. Measured on `coordinator-4950501b927a581b666a974cb9ce21b5`
(2026-11-15 15:58 to 11-18 02:03, 73 assistant Turns): 950 model rounds, 1,383
tool calls, 326 M prompt tokens (about 344 K per round), USD 567, 8 compaction
archives (5 window, 3 checkpoint). Of its tool results, `poll` was 436 calls /
2.8 MB, `get_conversation` 58 calls / 1.7 MB (about 23 KB each, every one a
read of a Worker conversation's last Turn) and `get_project_assignment` 24
calls. Each `get_project_assignment` produced 0.6 to 23 MB raw (median 3 MB):
the view copied the Worker Turn's whole projection (tool rounds, API rounds,
timing traces), and its 12 KB model view held only the brief and the frozen
`request.delegation` policy (every Worker and Coordinator tool name), never
reaching the execution, review or delivery it was read for. Polls cut results
at 4,000 characters (306 of 405 news rows), and the tool description told the
lead to follow a nonexistent `target_conversation_id` with `get_conversation`
for outcomes: so a lead learned each result three times, partially each time.
The poll instruction also repeated every ID its structured fields listed
(297 KB of the 2.8 MB).

Decision: a lead's view is a projection fixed in the contract.
`coordinatorRequest` keeps what was asked, of whom and under which checks
(brief, acceptance, model, budget, dependencies, rework, attachments,
authorization source); the frozen grant (`profile`, `delegation`) is admission
authority the lead never acts on and is omitted. `coordinatorResult` keeps the
Worker's final answer and the background output delivered into its Turn
(`content`, `_bgCommandInjects`); telemetry stays in the Worker conversation.
Every `view_for_coordinator` (assignment reads, review and cancel receipts,
poll news) uses both, so the view no longer grows with the Worker's rounds
(specification: 3,526 B at 1 round and at 500; 1,073,166 B at 500 before). A
poll's news row carries the whole `result`, since it is reported exactly once;
the instruction names fields (`workers`, `pending.running`,
`pending.delivering`, `unchanged`, phase `held`) instead of IDs (replayed over
the 429 polls: 296,219 to 171,617 instruction bytes).

Alternatives rejected: a larger excerpt or a per-call `max_chars` (a tuned
size, principle 5, and still a second read for long answers); returning the
result by artifact reference only (a mandatory second round for the one thing
the lead polled for); dropping `result` from news and keeping
`get_project_assignment` as the only reader (every settlement would cost a
round). Human-facing history pages keep their own `historyRow` projection;
exact human lookups keep the complete view.

Remaining measured bottlenecks, ranked by lead time lost, with their owner:
1. Publication blocks. 66 of 135 checkpointed accepts were reported held at
   least once: `project_publication_authority_missing` 56 rows (the
   delivery-registry defect, owned by its own assignment), `task_delta_conflict`
   13, `task_delta_entangled` 3, `git checkout-index failed` 3, `workspace_busy`
   2. Accept to canonical as the lead saw it: p50 50 s, p90 923 s, max 2,467 s.
   Fixed (follow-up e11fb172): landings were already serial per project, so
   each merges onto its predecessor's result; insertions both sides made at one
   place now keep both, the earlier landing's first, and a real conflict reaches
   the lead as `delivery.conflict` with its exact files and a ready rework brief.
   A landed delivery records its checkpoint as a second parent, so a superseded
   assignment whose checkpoint another landing (or `git_land`) contains settles
   as delivered without review. Decision and replay: "Landings in sequence keep
   both insertions" in `docs/modules/project_integration.md`.
2. Worker startup. `worker_entry` for Workers: p50 2.1 s, p95 44.5 s, max
   131 s (153 starts, `[TaskStartupTiming]` in app.log 11-17..18), nearly all
   `workspace_root_binding`; a fresh materialize took 129 s once
   (`workspace_metadata` 126 s), resumed bindings 21 to 26 s, of which
   `workspace_bind` about 12 s and two untimed checkout identity reads. The
   same reads take 14 to 70 ms in a fresh process on this host (load average
   64 to 145 during the measurement), so this is host contention, which
   principle 3 forbids gating. Removed (e11fb172): the second `_checkout` of
   the bound workspace, a Git process that re-read the identity the first read
   had verified; the binding compares its own fields instead. Fixture medians
   (8 binds each, load 10 to 12): resumed 58 to 46 ms, fresh 253 to 240 ms; one
   identity read costs 15.5 ms on this host at load 11 (live gain under the
   measured load 64 to 145 not measured;
   `test_binding_reads_the_task_checkouts_identity_once_fresh_or_resumed`).
3. Waiting. Polls spent 40.3 of 41.9 tool-wall hours; 22 timeouts of 1,500 s
   (nothing settled) and 44 `pending_input` returns (p50 122 s) on 11-17..18.
   These are idle waits on Workers, not lost lead work.
4. Duplicated work (measured e11fb172 over the lead's 162 delegations,
   11-15..17, against delivery commits on the shared branch): 21 were
   "Merge-repair"/"Re-land" Workers redoing a held landing by hand (10 on
   11-15, 4 on 11-16, 7 on 11-17); the causes were the delta-base and
   generated-output defects (fixed 11-15/16), publication-authority holds
   (sibling assignment) and same-place insertions (fixed by the landing rule
   above). Of 95 assignments with a landed delivery, 57 concurrently running
   pairs touched the same non-generated file, 32 of them a code file (most
   shared: `docs/PROJECT_COORDINATION.md` 21 pairs,
   `frontend/src/runtime/app-runtime.js` 6). Same-goal duplicates by title
   while both ran: 2 pairs of the same re-land started twice (9b1975d4 /
   c210a6c9, 8cc1d672 / 1b432251) and 1 overlapping goal split across two
   Workers (cdc1c942 / 4abd0466, the ▶ chevron replacement). File overlap
   between concurrent Workers is ordinary and now lands by itself; the
   re-land duplicates disappear with the repair Workers they duplicated.

Verify `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_evidence_envelope.py`.

Decision (2026-11-12): `poll` absorbed `await_project_workers`. Problem: two
blocking waits answered one question (block on handles; end early on Stop, input
or a deadline): `poll` for commands, swarm agents and tasks, and
`await_project_workers` for Workers, with different argument names (`wait_s`,
`timeout_seconds`), defaults (a read, a full wait), caps (1,500 s, 1,800 s) and
two copies of the pending-input rule. The copies had drifted: the cross-process
steer fix ("Cross-process steer delivery" below) reached the Worker wait but not
`poll`, so an ordinary Turn executing in the execution process stayed blind to a
message the API process accepted until its handle finished or its cap passed.
`wait_for` also made the model choose a settlement: a delivery wait on an
unreviewed Worker ran to its timeout, an execution wait on an accepted one
returned at once. Decision: one rule ends every `poll` wait
(`lib/tasks_pkg/wait_interruption.py`); `poll` routes a Coordinator's handles to
its Workers; what a Worker settles at follows its own state, so `wait_for` is
gone. The Coordinator's `poll` is described for its Workers
(`coordinator_poll_tool`: the same name, parameters and validation), and `kill`
stays outside its ceiling: `cancel_project_work` stops a Worker.
`await_project_workers` stays dispatchable as the coordinator family's
compatibility entry, the same wait. Name repair routes a fresh call to `poll`,
which first takes every retired wait spelling as its own (`timeout_seconds`,
`timeout`, `agent_ids`, `assignment_ids`): schema defaults filled `wait_s: 0`
and turned each such repaired wait into a read. A continuation policy frozen
before this change names `await_project_workers`; `scope.granted_tools` reads
it as `poll`, which reaches the same Workers. The 1,500 s cap replaces 1,800 s;
Worker settlement also resumes the Coordinator. Alternatives rejected: sharing
only the interruption code under two tools (two vocabularies and defaults for
one concept remain); one project tool joining `get_project_workers` and the
wait (a project-only copy of `poll`); `wait_for` on `poll` (an argument for one
handle family that the model must choose right); a waiting default for `poll`
(every role's documented read). Verify `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_workers.py`,
the committed-steer case of `tests/tasks_pkg/test_poll_kill.py`, the frozen
policy in `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_continuation.py` and
`tests/tools/test_coordinator_argument_repair.py`.
Problem: N11 — six Turns were force-failed as `worker_lost` while a Coordinator
waited (11-04 22:32, 23:40; 11-05 01:23; 11-06 11:40, 11:53, 12:34). The first
three were inside `await_project_workers` on a boot predating the per-poll
heartbeat above (live since 11-05 09:59); that heartbeat is the fix, so a
responsive wait is never reaped. The last three were inside
`delegate_project_work`, undeclared synchronous dispatch under host overload —
not a wait at all, and not made safe by any wait marker. Decision: keep the
heartbeat as the await's only liveness evidence; a `_delegationWaits` marker
whose freshness equals the stall threshold would be redundant while polls
complete and would double a genuinely blocked status read's reap, because the
reaper restarts silence from the last explained verdict.
Problem (2026-11-09): `delegate_project_work` prepared the 1.4M-file plain
folder of the autoharness project inside the call (`require_isolated_worker_root`
runs `prepare_managed_repository`: whole-tree `ls-files`, a per-path content walk
and `add -A`) for 6,931 s; the reaper force-failed the Coordinator at 1,836 s as
`worker_lost` and the delegation's late result was lost. Decision: preparation
reports every observed advance (a Git tree the kernel shows executing, a completed
command, a walked path) to its caller, and delegation refreshes the Coordinator's
existing dispatch heartbeat with it, exactly as a completed status poll does;
Worker binding does the same for the Worker before its first round. A step that
stops advancing supplies nothing, so a stuck delegation is still reaped one
threshold after its last advance, and the Coordinator's Stop ends the preparation.
Alternatives rejected: a `_delegationWaits` marker or an unconditional exemption
hides a blocked handler (as for the await); raising the stall threshold is a knob
that only moves the false reap; preparing after delegation would create Workers
and grants for roots no Worker can use. Verification:
`test_a_long_root_preparation_keeps_its_delegating_coordinator_live` and
`test_a_root_preparation_that_stops_advancing_leaves_its_coordinator_reapable`
(`test_coordinator_workers.py`),
`test_a_workers_root_preparation_is_its_liveness_before_its_first_round`
(`test_project_dispatch_file_lifecycle.py`).
Problem (2026-11-11): a Worker's workspace admission
(`integration_control.create_task_workspace`: input snapshot, repository
admission, checkout) refreshed neither clock after worker entry. On the
contended data volume, 8 of that day's 19 admissions were force-failed at
1,800 s as "Task cancelled during workspace admission" while still advancing:
binding p50 1,950 s, of which checkout p50 646 s, snapshot p50 356 s, and up to
2,368 s queued for `_repo_lock`. Decision: admission reports observed advances
to the Worker's existing dispatch heartbeat (`workspace_delivery`), as root
preparation does: each poll while queued for the destination, which a live
process holds (admission has taken no repository admission since 2026-11-12,
"Task checkouts start at once" in `docs/modules/project_integration.md`); each
observed execution of the input capture's, a spare update's or the checkout's
Git commands; and the same steps when a missing checkout is restored. A
command blocked on the volume reports nothing, so an admission is still reaped
one threshold after its last advance, and the command's own stall window still
retires it. Alternatives rejected: an explained-wait marker for the whole
binding hides a blocked step; raising the threshold is a knob that only moves the
false reap. Verification: `tests/tasks_pkg/test_workspace_admission_liveness.py`.
A device command
claimed by a connected device publishes `_deviceWaits` and is `device` under
the bridge's own presence rule (`_command_active_locked`), which has no
observation clock of its own; an unclaimed command or a disconnected device
explains nothing. Verification: `test_explained_wait.py`,
`test_stuck_task_reaper.py`, `test_coordinator_workers.py`.
`review_project_work` pins the exact completed result; its `actor=coordinator`
never means human acceptance. An optional exact registered checkpoint requests
publication through existing integration gates. An accept whose delivery cannot
publish that checkpoint (the receipt records another attempt, an attempt that did
not complete, or a state the receiver never acts on) is
refused before the review is recorded, naming the receipt's state and reason
("Delivery follows the attempt that completes" in
[Project integration](modules/project_integration.md#task-owned-canonical-delivery)).
`poll` on an accepted Worker observes the real delivery receipt.

Problem: in a production Coordinator conversation these waits were 68% of wall
time. Waiting for all selected Workers held finished results until timeout (13
multi-Worker calls, late wake p50 137 s); delivery waits ignored blocked
receipts, so 25 of 26 timed out (5,517 s) while the cause sat in
`delivery.reason`; the notification-to-durable-status gap left single waits to
the 5 s boundary (3.4-6.1 s after completion); every poll read the whole Worker
Turn (2.28 MB for 121 rounds) to keep 4,000 characters. Decision: any-settle
return measured against the call-start state, held receipts settle delivery
waits, a bounded prompt recheck after notification, and settlement-only polls.
Alternatives rejected: returning while any selected Worker is settled (a call
that repeats a finished Worker returns at once, in a paid model loop); keeping
the all-settle wait behind a schema flag (the measured cost); counting only
canonical delivery (strands the Coordinator on causes only it or the human can
resolve); treating the in-memory notification as settlement (not durable
evidence); caching projections between polls (stale evidence, unbounded
memory). Known limit: a call that selects only held deliveries returns at once,
so a receipt that clears by itself (`workspace_busy`) is rechecked later, not
awaited alone. Verification: `test_coordinator_workers.py` and the native
`test_coordinator_workers_authority.py`.

Problem: a wait held the Coordinator turn for up to 1,800 s while checking only
Stop and Worker settlement, so a human steer, a forwarded request or a Worker's
question waited for the timeout; a Worker asking the Coordinator that awaits it
stalled until then. Decision: yield on the inbox lanes `poll` already yields on,
plus project messages newer than the call start, reusing the existing wait
slice and settlement recheck, as one outcome named like `poll`'s. Alternatives
rejected: shorter timeouts (a tuning knob, still blind to input); yielding only
to human chat messages (a forwarded request, a channel batch, a background
completion or a Worker's question would still wait for the timeout); a separate
outcome per input kind (the Coordinator acts the same way on each: read the
input, then wait on the named Workers); yielding to every unconsidered project message (one
that stays `submitted`, from an ineligible sender or an unconfirmed carrier,
would end every later wait at once in a paid model loop); a mailbox notification
lane or listener (passive project messages deliberately have no wake). Known
limit: a message submitted after the round acquired input but before the call
started arrives when the wait ends. Verification: `test_coordinator_workers.py`.

Problem: a resumed or later human-request Coordinator could accept an earlier
Worker's checkpoint but stamp publication with the reviewer's physical task.
The live publication gate correctly required the Worker's original delegation,
so legitimate review failed after retaining an unusable publication receipt.
Decision: review still validates the current caller, binding, original human
source, grant and exact result/checkpoint, then pins publication to the original
Worker delegation. An authorized repeat of the same review also repairs that
receipt's subject, preserving its original timestamp and all immutable pins.
No grant is enlarged or recreated. Alternatives rejected: loosening the live
publication gate admits unverifiable authority; requiring another human grant
breaks continuity for already-authorized work; preserving the bad subject on
replay strands existing receipts. `test_coordinator_publication_handoff.py`
covers physical resume, later human steering, retained-receipt repair and Stop.

`delegate_project_work` accepts the existing version-pinned `dependencies` and
`rework_of` shapes. They use the same dependency/review/publication admission;
no parallel graph executor is introduced. Pending review or delivery defers the
same grant, and changed result/checkpoint pins refuse execution. The graph reads
these durable links. `test_coordinator_dependency_links.py` exercises automatic
delegation, deferred replay, stale pins and exact rework links.
Unknown model/tool effects, source withdrawal and checkpoint changes still block
publication. A completed Worker or an accepted review alone never proves delivery.

Problem: a model `dispatch_intent` whose settlement observation was lost (CAS
exhaustion, storage outage, an ambiguous intent ACK whose void is refused, or a
restart) stayed durable forever, so publication refused a long-finished Worker
with an unnamed "In-flight paid execution" error. Decision: the publication gate,
lane settlement and startup recovery settle such an intent as an unknown-usage
lower bound only when the execution attempt is no longer live and this process
holds no open ExecutionBudget lease for that exact root/ordinal; a fold
watermark never hides a retained open row. Refusals name the blocking model or
tool occurrence. Alternatives rejected: voiding the intent (it may have been
billed), dropping the in-flight rule, or a timeout-based sweep (a slow live call
is not proof of an ended one). `test_project_dispatch_ended_intents.py` covers it.

The tool-receipt fold follows the same rule from the other side: calls settle out
of ordinal order (a long `run_command` beside a swarm's quick calls), and a root's
watermark folds every ordinal at or below it, so `fold_settled_tools` folds only
terminal rows below that root's lowest open ordinal. Problem: folding past an open
row made its completion read as already folded; the row stayed `intent` and fenced
every later effect of the Worker as an unconfirmed prior effect. Alternative
rejected: letting a folded-looking event update a retained row (the watermark would
still claim it settled). `test_project_dispatch_effect_fence.py` covers it.

### An unconfirmed effect never wedges a Worker (2026-11-11 decision)

Problem: a tool occurrence's intent is durable before its handler runs; its
completion is written afterwards. A storage outage swallowed one completion,
the process died, and the restart resumed the same ledger. From then on, every
effectful call and every platform read (`poll`, `await_project_workers`,
`get_conversation`, `search_tools`, `inspect_tool_effect`, `integration_checkpoint`)
refused "Prior tool effect remains unconfirmed". Continue, checkpoint_resume,
regenerate and an unreviewed follow_up kept the ledger, and publication refused
too: 93 refusals in 14 conversations, two Workers looping on them.

Decision (owner-approved):
- (a) When an attempt continues an account (`bind_account`, `continued`), every
  open intent of its root belongs to a settled predecessor. It settles as the
  terminal phase `interrupted_effect_unknown` before the attempt admits a call,
  and a `lifecycle_recovery` note names each call (tool, ordinal, call ID,
  operation ID) and says to verify its effect before repeating it.
- (b) In unbounded project/Coordinator grants, a call whose registered
  declaration is read-only or idempotent neither waits on nor opens a fence
  (`ExecutionBudget.bind_model_attempt_observer(observe_repeatable_tools=False)`).
  A built-in's registered declaration decides (`declared_effect_recovery`):
  gateway schemas compile a conservative reconcile document, yet each inner call
  of `execute_tools`/`execute_program` is fenced at its own entry. Dynamic
  MCP/custom tools use the request's reviewed contract. Declarations are
  load-bearing, so `tests/tools/test_tool_effect_declarations.py` audits every
  registered one; `integration_checkpoint`/`integration_submit` were declared
  reads although they create checkpoint refs, and are now non-cacheable
  idempotent effects.
- (c) A completion whose write fails is retained, not swallowed. The next
  observed call writes it first and is refused only while it still cannot be
  written; repeatable calls and the root's settlement retry it best-effort.
  A process that dies with it unwritten leaves it to (a).
- (d) The refusal names the occurrence's `operation_id` (a digest of its
  immutable identity, `accounting.tool_operation_id`) and producing Turn.
  `inspect_tool_effect` with that ID settles the occurrence of any tool as
  `effect_unknown_acknowledged` (outcome `unknown`, reason `acknowledged`).
  Either unknown phase yields to a later real outcome of the same occurrence.
- (e) Publication, `finish_project_request` and dependency readiness ignore an
  open intent of a read-only or idempotent tool; a side effect blocks until
  (a) or (d) settles it, and a phase this code does not know still blocks
  (`accounting.unsettled_tool_effect`).
- A refusal proven before handler entry settles its occurrence `not_entered`.

Alternatives rejected: reusing `executor_exception` for (a), which would
conflate an executor's own failure with an interruption nobody observed; a
timeout or startup sweep that settles side effects nobody is told about;
exempting reads in finite grants, whose ledger is their conserved call budget
(they keep observing every call); and accepting the gateway compile's
reconcile default for its own outer call, which fences a Worker on a wrapper
whose inner effects are already fenced. Specs:
`tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_effect_fence.py` and the publication
and dependency cases in `test_project_dispatch_ended_intents.py` and
`test_project_dispatch_dependencies.py`.

Physical retries/resumes of the same accepted human input retain one source
cohort, idempotency identity, first issued Worker policy and shared allowance.
They recover, review and finish the earlier Workers; a new physical attempt
cannot hide unfinished work or reset its spending. A new human message can read
`get_project_workers(scope=project)` through bounded history pages and review or
cancel earlier Workers in the same live owner/tenant/Coordinator binding epoch.
Returned source Turn/current-request facts distinguish earlier objectives. Review
still requires live original authority and exact completed evidence; cancellation
never renews a grant. Synthetic continuations can read that history but control
only their original request. The alternatives—attempt-local status, treating a
new message as an empty project, or giving every synthetic continuation human
steering authority—lose continuity or cross the original authorization boundary.
`test_coordinator_worker_handoff.py` and its three-authority storage replay verify
resume/replay, completion, human steering, bounded history and synthetic refusal.

### Worker workspace preflight

Problem: Workers run in registered isolated Git worktrees, but nothing made a
project root able to host one. In a plain folder (the RSI Exam project: notes,
task packages and about 3.6 GB of data, never under Git) `delegate_project_work`
still created conversations and grants; each Worker paid for a model round, then
`admit_tool` refused its first tool call, and that refusal settled as
`interrupted/user_abort` with its reason erased, so the Coordinator reported a
phantom Stop. A first fix refused such roots early with `git init`/`git commit`
instructions; the owner rejected that, because the people using these folders
never run Git (decision 2026-10-25).

Decision: Tofu prepares the folder itself. `workspace_delivery.isolated_workspace_refusal`
first calls `managed_repository.prepare_managed_repository`, then checks the one
root contract: the accepted project path resolves to the real top level of a primary
local checkout with at least one commit. Preparation turns a plain folder, or an
unborn repository with an empty index, into a Tofu-managed repository: one
baseline commit by `Tofu Integration` with a matching index (the folder reads
clean), `tofu.managedRepository=true`, and a create-only default `AGENTS.md`
when the folder has no root guide (`CLAUDE.md`, `.cursorrules`, `AGENTS.md`,
`COPILOT.md`). User files are never modified, moved or deleted. The repository
is assembled in a `.tofu_git_init_*` directory and appears as `.git` only when
complete; a crashed attempt is discarded by the next one; preparation that may
create or refresh a managed repository holds the per-root
`integration_control._repo_lock`. A primary repository with its own committed
history is read-only: recognition and `user_repository` return never take that
mutating-admission lock, so dispatch does not queue behind unrelated snapshots
or publication. A managed repository's refresh still revalidates under the lock.
Its whole-tree Git (`ls-files`,
`add -A`, `read-tree`, `write-tree`, `check-ignore`) has no total deadline: like
the integration snapshot it runs under the shared `GIT_STALL_SECONDS` window
([project integration](modules/project_integration.md)), so a large folder takes
as long as its volume needs and only a Git tree that stopped executing for a whole
window fails the preparation as a typed `volume_stalled` refusal. The caller
observes each advance as its liveness (above) and its Stop ends the preparation; a
failed or stopped preparation leaves the folder as it was. The content policy
lives in `.git/tofu-managed.json` and a managed section of `.git/info/exclude`,
which linked worktrees share:

- never versioned: runtime, dependency and cache trees (`FORBIDDEN_CHECKPOINT_*`),
  `.tofu*` artifacts and the root `.venv` (bridged per checkout by `workspace_runtime`);
- shared: files of at least 10 MiB, untracked directories of at least 2,000 files
  that are mostly not source text, virtual environments, nested repositories,
  paths the folder's own ignore files hide (`git check-ignore` decides, so the
  parent of a shared path is never mistaken for one), root runtime directories
  and, once the owner chose so, nested projects (see the next paragraph).
  Worker binding (`workspace_delivery._binding`) links them into every checkout
  (`link_shared_paths`); workers read them in place, file tools refuse writes
  through them because the target resolves to the canonical checkout, project
  search skips links, and reclamation treats exact links as reconstructible;
- everything else is versioned: isolated, reviewed and published as before.

**Large folders and nested projects (2026-11-09).** A project manifest at the
folder root (`managed_repository_discovery.PROJECT_MANIFESTS`: each ecosystem's
build/package manifest, not dependency lists or generic `Makefile`s) makes the
root the project and preparation proceeds as above. Otherwise
`managed_repository_discovery.discover` lists the folder top-down and stops at
nested Git repositories, virtual environments, nested projects and paths the
folder's ignore files hide (`ActivitySupervisedExchange` keeps one
check-ignore answering per path, so an ignored directory is never listed);
their paths are excluded before any Git walk, so Git never enters them either.
A directory that holds nothing but nested projects is one shared folder of them.
The owner can additionally name whole folders of read-only run/reference data
(`shared_folders=...`, recorded as `sharedFolders`): they are shared whole and
never entered, which is how a project whose `work/` holds runtime distributions,
wheels, bundles and reports avoids versioning them.
Each listing is appended to a discovery journal in
`integration_control._control_root()/preparations/` (never in the folder), so an
interrupted or refused preparation resumes where it stopped; the journal is
discarded once preparation succeeds, and a mismatched header or torn record
discards only what follows it. When such a folder holds projects with a manifest
of their own, only the owner can say whether they belong to this project, so
preparation refuses with `ambiguous_project_root` instead of guessing: its
`details` carry the candidates grouped by top-level folder, the question, the
options and the recommended answer (`shared`), and nothing is written into the
folder and no Worker exists. The answer is supplied as
`prepare_managed_repository(nested_projects=...)`, recorded as `nestedProjects`
in the manifest, and never overridden by a later call (`decision_recorded`).
`scripts/prepare_project_folder.py` reports a folder read-only
(`preview_preparation`) and applies the owner's answer.

Measurement (autoharness on DolphinFS, 1,397,250 files, read-only dry run):
discovery listed 37,631 directories in 65 s instead of walking and listing 1.4M
paths, and refused with the typed question (21,673 nested projects: `work/`
21,668, `reference/` 5); resuming from the journal took 8.7 s. Sharing nested
projects alone would still version 21,825 files (694 MiB) of runtime
distributions, wheels, bundles and reports (192 s). Sharing `work/` and
`reference/` as the owner's data folders instead versions 89 files (7.4 MiB)
with 4 shared paths in about 12 s. A folder with a project manifest at its root
and bulky data is still prepared in one pass with its data shared.

`preview_preparation` and the script write only into their scratch directory;
nothing is created, changed or removed in the folder during a dry run. The
script applies the owner's answers without Git:
`python3 scripts/prepare_project_folder.py <folder> --nested-projects shared
--shared-folder work --shared-folder reference --apply`.

Every preflight refreshes the policy, so newly added bulky data (and nested
projects, under a recorded `shared` answer) joins the shared set, which only
grows; then it commits what the folder holds, because Workers start from its
branch (owner decision 2026-11-12, "Task checkouts start at once" in
`docs/modules/project_integration.md`). Data that appears between preflights,
a Worker's outputs in its own checkout above all, is classified by the same
policy at every staging and withheld unread; a checkout holding such data is
never reclaimed ("Staging withholds what the content policy never versions" in
`docs/modules/project_integration.md`). A repository with its own history is
changed once, at its first delegation: its working tree is committed and
`tofu.linearCheckpoint` set, unless its owner set that to `false`. Remaining refusals carry a `code`
naming the cause and a bilingual remedy without Git commands: a subdirectory of
another repository, a linked worktree, a missing or remote root, an unwritable
folder, an unborn repository with staged files (Tofu does not commit someone
else's staged work), an ambiguous project root (above) and a stalled volume.
Delegation calls the check through `admission.require_isolated_worker_root`
before creating any conversation or grant (accepted replays are not rechecked)
and returns a refusal with `execution_started: false`, the typed `code`, its
`details` and do-not-retry recovery (an ambiguous root names the owner question
instead). Worker binding repeats it for files/project grants before workspace
creation or the first model request, failing through `task_start_failed` with
the remedy as its hint, so the same cause reaches the Coordinator when the
delegation ran on another host. Late `admit_tool` refusals raise
`ToolAdmissionRefused` and settle as failed
(`docs/contracts/TURN_SETTLEMENT.md`). A Worker `poll` adds the settled
`error` (kind/detail/hint) for failed, interrupted or truncated Workers.

Measurement (RSI Exam on DolphinFS, Git 2.34, dry run against a scratch Git
directory): first preparation 15 s (content walk 10.7 s, baseline 3.6 s);
1,143 versioned files and a 21 MB `.git`; four shared paths (27,125 Qlib `.bin`
files and three parquet panels of 93 to 331 MiB) instead of about 28,000 more
versioned files and 720 MiB copied into every checkout; `git status` 0.1 s and
clean afterwards.

Alternatives rejected: refusing with Git instructions (the people who own these
folders never run Git); running Workers directly in the canonical folder (loses
isolation, review and publication); versioning everything (checkout cost on
network volumes grows with file count, and every Worker would copy the data);
a separate non-Git snapshot/publication store (a second delivery authority);
committing someone else's staged index; guidance that overwrites a person's
file. For large folders: a file-count cap, size budget or timeout (the owner
rules out caps, and a cap only moves the failure); guessing which nested
projects are part of the project (a manifest-less monorepo and a folder of
copies look alike, so only the owner decides, once and recorded); sharing
nested projects unconditionally (would silently make a project's own packages
read-only to workers); preparing into a worker checkout before the owner
answered (writes into the folder and creates a Worker that cannot work).
Verification: `test_managed_repository.py`,
`test_managed_repository_large_folders.py` (typed ambiguity with an untouched
folder, both owner answers, pruned discovery, resume, typed stall, read-only
preview), `test_activity_supervised_process.py`,
`test_project_dispatch_file_lifecycle.py` (plain folder to isolated write,
review and publication back into the folder), `test_workspace_completion.py`,
`test_coordinator_workers.py` (the owner's question as the delegation's tool
result) and `test_tool_admission_refusal_verdict.py`.

Problem: rejecting a project path solely because it contains a symlink forced
people to reopen the same folder and resend their request, even though the real
directory was already known. Decision: resolve local project aliases (including
symlinked ancestors) inside preparation and workspace admission. The original
project key, Coordinator/Worker conversations and accepted grant remain unchanged;
Git operations, locks and workspace registration use the resolved physical root.
Dispatch ownership and delivery receipt lookups resolve the grant's project path
at the filesystem boundary and still require the exact owner, task, registered
workspace and captured canonical root. Retargeting an alias cannot transfer an
existing Worker's ownership or publication to another directory. Broken aliases,
repository subdirectories and linked worktrees retain their existing refusals.
Alternatives rejected: reopening/rekeying the project loses conversation and
authorization continuity; merely removing the preflight refusal leaves physical
workspace lookups mismatched; bypassing isolation weakens the delivery contract.
The alias variants of the preparation, workspace refusal, Coordinator delegation
and file lifecycle tests verify this path through isolated write and publication.

### A hold only its assignment can lift is decided once per row

Problem: the delivery receiver revisits every receipt that asks for action on
each pass and drains a project's rows back to back on its lane. On 2026-11-12,
149 publication holds (77 revoked grants, 71 awaiting a review) and 50
delivered rows whose reclaim waits on publication were re-validated live on
every pass, for good: 16% of the execution host's dispatch reads, plus a Git
diff per row and pass. Making each visit cheaper would only make the lane loop
faster.

Decision: `publication._publication_verdict` remembers, per workspace row, a
refusal only the assignment can lift: `DispatchGrantRevoked` (never lifted) or
`PublicationAwaitsReview` (no accepting review and pin; lifted only by a review
or publish, whose commit paths call `release_publication_hold`). The entry is
keyed by the row's fingerprint (state, checkpoint, workspace and origin,
including its receipt), so the receiver's `_resume_row` skips an unchanged
held row, and a delivered row whose checkpoint has a delta, without reading
storage. The row's identity (owner, task, and the project its root resolves to
now) is still checked on every visit, before the memo: an alias can move
without changing the row (`test_project_dispatch_file_lifecycle.py`, symlink and
ancestor cases). Git's answer for two immutable commits is remembered per row
(`task_delta_delivery.remembered_no_delta`). A release attempt forgets its row before deciding,
and again when it still refuses; a recovery pass (startup, worker error)
forgets every row; any write to the row changes its fingerprint. Every other
refusal (transient read failures, a changed result revision, scope) keeps its
per-pass retry, and a remembered refusal never permits publication.

A review or publish committed in a process other than the receiver's, whose
release then fails, leaves its row held until the next recovery pass or review;
formerly the next pass would have retried it.

Alternatives rejected: rereading the assignment's version on every visit (one
read per held row and pass, still bound to the lane's loop); excluding these
holds from the storage action filter (a new receipt field in both
authorities). Revoked holds no longer stay held: see the next decision.

### A delivery its assignment can never publish closes (2026-11-18)

Problem: on 2026-11-18 the tofu, knowledge, parallel and router projects held 96
receipts as `blocked` (`project_publication_authority_missing`, 60) or `preserved`
("Task did not complete; checkpoint retained", 36). 71 belonged to cancelled
assignments and 23 to results reviewed `reject` or `rework` (18 reworks had a
successor, 9 already delivered). None could ever publish, yet views, polls and the
Router showed them as holds, and every recovery pass decided them again. The two
that did need a lead (a result never reviewed, an accept recorded without
`checkpoint_sha`) belonged to finished requests, whose leads no wake told.

Decision:
- `validate_publication` raises `PublicationClosed` (cause `cancelled`, `rejected`,
  `reworked`) for a recorded grant revocation or a reject/rework review; a Stop or
  lapsed owner delegation stays `DispatchGrantRevoked`. Each is final: a revoked
  grant never returns and a reject/rework is replaced only by a rework.
- The delivery owner then settles the receipt `closed` (`delivery._close`, reason
  `project_publication_closed: assignment <cause>; ...`), terminal like
  `delivered`/`preserved` and outside both authorities' action filter (which asks
  only for `working`, `pending`, `blocked`). The checkpoint and its refs stay, so a
  successor can still merge it; a checkpoint the branch already contains still
  settles delivered. An incomplete attempt of a cancelled assignment closes instead
  of preserving.
- `cancel_project_work`, the revoke API, an unadopted birth's withdrawal and a
  reject/rework review release the row (`service._close_final_hold`), so the hold
  closes at once; older rows close on the next recovery pass, and `delivery_view`
  already reports a final assignment's legacy hold as `closed`. `worker_phase`
  treats `closed` as settled.
- A continuation lists, besides its own request's Workers, every Worker of the same
  binding whose result awaits review or was accepted without a pin while its
  workspace receipt is not final (`unpinned`: inspect the assignment before
  deciding whether publication is needed; result acceptance alone requests none).

Known evidence gap: that visibility requires a readable, nonfinal delivery view.
`delivery_view=None` conflates missing/mismatched workspace or receipt evidence
with unavailable reads. `worker_phase` currently treats an accepted result with
that value as settled, so it can be omitted from continuation context; this is
not canonical-delivery proof. Ordinary `project` grants all carry writable `.`
scope, including explanation-only work, and registration establishes ownership,
not whether the result requires file delivery. A `delivered/no_changes` receipt
is positive delivery evidence, not a non-file intent discriminator.

No read-only repair can distinguish these cases from current structured intent.
The smallest missing producer input is a durable delivery-applicability value
(`required` or `not_applicable`, absence remaining unknown), frozen by delegation
on the request independently of the workspace receipt. This is a proposed input,
not an implemented field or an inferred result; it would not itself prove delivery
or authorize publication. Until that authority exists, keep phase/wake behavior
unchanged rather than guess from briefs, review notes, capabilities, or missing
checkpoints. The isolated diagnostic
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_delivery_intent_diagnosis.py` reproduces the
same absent view for completed, accepted ordinary file/explanation briefs with
absent workspace, registered workspace without a receipt, and unavailable reads.
It verifies projection reads leave fixture state unchanged; it is not a red/green
repair or evidence of deployed behavior.

The current optional producer negotiation stops at the closed
`delegateRequest` and `request` definitions in
`contracts/project_dispatch_v1.schema.json`: neither declares a request-owned
metadata extension. `coordinator_workers.delegate` validates the arguments;
`authorize_from_coordinator` validates the constructed request; existing readers
validate stored nested requests in both legacy and segmented records. Thus adding
an undeclared applicability member would break those readers, not provide a
schema-free compatible extension. The internal ledger mutation port also forbids
changing the immutable request. Open tool/config or prompt dictionaries are not
delivery-metadata owners and must not carry a shadow applicability record.

`tests/storage/test_coordinator_delivery_applicability_boundary.py` makes this
stop executable through ordinary synthetic delegation adapters and disposable
SQLite storage, for both legacy and segmented dispatch records. It probes explicit
`required`/`not_applicable` and malformed values at argument/request admission,
checks refusal leaves durable and execution state unchanged, and rereads omitted
metadata after current-result review with absent/fresh delivery receipts. Worker
summaries and the secondary Coordinator service view agree without writing state.
The candidate field spellings in those refusal probes define no supported API.
Request closure alone does not exclude an envelope carrier. The existing
segmented storage contract accepts object-valued versioned envelopes;
`SegmentedDispatch` retains unknown envelope annotations through ordinary record
updates. The same test file now demonstrates that retention durably on SQLite
for both proposed values, including a domain-validated current-result review.
It also replaces the annotation after review with an invalid object: repository
reads still accept the unchanged nested record. Thus storage compatibility is
supported here, but applicability ownership/validation and immutable initial
capture are not. Ordinary delegation creates no such annotation, repository
reads project only the nested record, and the `metadata` argument is an internal
serialization parameter, not a declared request-authoring port. The storage
contract explicitly assigns domain validation and immutable authority to the
repository; accepting JSON is not authorization to assign it delivery semantics.

The exact stop is therefore at domain admission: neither the closed request
ports nor the retaining envelope currently supplies an admitted, validated,
request-frozen applicability value. Using a raw storage annotation as that value
would introduce an arbitrary field, which this narrow task excludes. A future
negotiation may choose a declared envelope owner instead of changing the request
body, but must define authorized initial capture, validation, immutable linkage,
reader exposure and compatibility before a consumer treats it as applicability.
These tests do not prove such an extension impossible or require a request-schema
change specifically. No diagnostic, migration/default, publication, finish,
wake/retry, permission or automatic applicability claim is introduced.
Expiry/revocation behavior and cross-engine metadata retention are not extended
or certified by these probes.

Alternatives rejected: an age cutoff that expires holds (a knob, and an unreviewed
result is not stale by age); deleting receipts or checkpoints (loses what a
successor merges); a new receipt field in the storage filter (`closed` already
falls outside it). Verify `tests/project_mod/test_dead_delivery_holds.py`.

Specs: `tests/project_mod/test_project_publication.py`
(`test_a_hold_only_its_assignment_can_lift_is_decided_once_until_released`,
`test_a_hold_for_any_other_cause_is_decided_on_every_pass`).

### A publication hold names its cause

Problem: `_publication_verdict` held every failure to read the assignment's
authority as `project_publication_authority_missing`, and every failed
checkpoint diff as `project_publication_checkpoint_unavailable`. A Coordinator
wait settles on any `blocked` delivery and tells the Coordinator to act on its
reason (`coordinator_workers._settled`). So a storage outage, dispatch
contention or a Git timeout asked for a review that could change nothing, and a
Tofu defect (a `KeyError`, a storage integrity fault) read as missing authority.
From 2026-11-12 23:35 to 2026-11-13 02:31, three of eleven logged holds were Git
diffs that timed out after 30 s, reported as an unavailable checkpoint.

Decision: the verdict classifies a failure by its type, never by its message.

- The assignment's verdicts keep `project_publication_authority_missing`.
  These are `DispatchGrantRevoked` and `PublicationAwaitsReview` (remembered per
  row, as above), the repository's other refusals (`PermissionError`,
  `ProjectDispatchError`), and storage answering `database_not_found` or
  `database_forbidden`.
- A read that did not answer is `project_publication_check_unavailable`. This
  covers `DispatchContention`, a transient storage code
  (`agent_core.storage_errors.is_transient_storage_error`), and a Git call whose
  `IntegrationError` is chained to a timeout or to a spawn error delivery already
  retries (`EAGAIN`, `EINTR`, `ENOMEM`). It is never remembered. The receiver's
  existing tick, due while the last pass returned rows, decides it again and
  publishes on the first pass after the read answers. No new review, accept or
  publish is needed. `delivery_view` shows such a hold as `pending`, so
  Coordinator waits keep waiting.
- Git's own refusal, such as a missing object, stays
  `project_publication_checkpoint_unavailable`.
- Anything else is `project_publication_check_defect: <exception type>`, with
  the code appended for a `StorageError`. It is logged at ERROR with its
  traceback once per cause, and retried each pass like every unremembered hold.

Alternatives rejected:

- Remembering transient holds. They would wait for an assignment commit that
  never comes.
- A durable cause field in the receipt and the view. That is a contract change,
  while the reason already carries up to 1,024 characters.
- Classifying `validate_publication`'s free-text `ProjectDispatchError` refusals
  by message: an in-flight model call, an unconfirmed tool effect, unready
  dependencies. Typed subclasses in `repository.py` are the follow-up that gives
  each its own reason.

Specs: `tests/project_mod/test_publication_hold_causes.py`.

### Project-grant publication fence (2026-11 decision)

Problem: publication (`lib/project_dispatch/publication.py`) applied the
file-grant path rule of `lib/project_dispatch/scope.py` to `project` checkpoints.
That rule refuses a path when any segment is `data`, `logs` or `uploads`. A
project grant pins `writePaths: ["."]`, and publication checks every old and new
path, so 43 tracked source files could never be added, changed, renamed or deleted
by a Worker. They are the model catalog in `lib/model_info/data/`,
`lib/site_packs/data/` and the Android `com/tofu/client/data` package. Delivery
stopped at `project_publication_write_scope_exceeded`.

Decision: the fence depends on the grant mode.

- Root `data/`, `logs/` and `uploads/` are refused for every grant, in any letter
  case.
- Publication passes `project_grant` for `mode: project`. A project checkpoint may
  then change a nested package with one of those names. This is the project-mode
  intent that `lib/git_checkpoint_policy.py` states for `FORBIDDEN_CHECKPOINT_ROOTS`:
  "Root-owned runtime/reconstructible trees. A nested source package stays legal."
- Build-output names (`build`, `dist`, `target`) follow the same split. A project
  checkpoint is a commit already filtered by the project's own `.gitignore` and
  `git_checkpoint_policy`, so tracked content there (for example a rendered
  `docs/report/build/` deck) is source; refusing it held whole paper/merge
  deliveries at `project_publication_write_scope_exceeded` despite `writePaths: ["."]`.
- File grants keep refusing these names at any depth. This covers descriptor I/O,
  profile prefixes, shared files and file-grant publication.
- Both modes refuse the following at any depth: checkout control, undo evidence,
  Tofu artifacts, dependency caches (`node_modules`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`), and
  `.env`/`.env.*`. The `.env.*`
  rule includes `.env.example`, because the fence cannot tell a template from a
  filled-in credential file.
- Refusals stay `ValueError` and name the refused root tree or segment. A held
  publication logs the first refused path once (`[ProjectPublication]` warning).

Measured over every tracked file under `.`: in project mode, 43 files become
publishable and two stay refused (`.env.example` and `uploads/images/.gitkeep`).
File mode is unchanged.

Alternatives rejected:

- Root-anchoring `data`, `logs` and `uploads` for all grants. This weakens
  file-grant confinement, whose specification refuses `src/data/secrets` and
  `src/logs/app.log`.
- Allowlisting the affected files. Every new nested `data` package would repeat
  the incident.
- Reusing `FORBIDDEN_CHECKPOINT_ROOTS` wholesale. That would add refusals for
  `output/` and the evaluation trees, which is a separate decision.

Known residue: the any-depth names still refuse Bazel `BUILD` files, Nx
`project.json` files and committed `dist/` trees in other projects. None is
tracked here.

Specifications:

- `test_project_dispatch_scope.py`: file grants refuse nested names and project
  grants accept them. Both modes refuse root runtime trees, credentials and
  metadata.
- `test_project_publication.py`: project grants deliver nested `data` packages,
  including renames. Root runtime trees and `.env*` files that a Worker commits
  stay blocked. File-grant publication still refuses nested names.

### No spending ceiling (2026-10 decision)

Problem: each request used to share 2,000,000 tokens, 128 model requests, 4,096
tool calls and two hours, with at most 16 model requests per Worker. In real use
(`coordinator-1a390889…`) every Worker and continuation stopped within minutes on
"previous model usage is unconfirmed"; unconfirmed attempts kept their whole
reservation, so the request reported "Coordinator needs a new budget" after a
handful of failures. The user had to return and re-authorize repeatedly, which
defeats handing off the work.

Decision: Coordinator requests, Workers and continuations carry no token, model
request, tool-call or time ceiling. `budget` is `{}` and profiles omit
`maxToolCalls` on new grants; their `executionLimits` are all null, so
`ExecutionBudget` admits without a finite cap and a missing provider receipt is a
recorded lower bound instead of a fence. The dispatch CAS still records every
physical occurrence exactly once (`accounting.py`) and `request_budget.py` keeps
the per-request account for identity, resume continuity and usage projection.
Earlier finite grants keep their recorded spend ceilings. Worker parallelism
has no task-count or memory ceiling. New policies omit `maxConcurrent`; older
issued values remain immutable receipt data and never refuse new Workers.
The grant CAS still checks source-policy identity, and Stop, binding,
human-source and exact-attempt authority remain on every model/tool call.

Alternatives rejected: an optional limit that is off by default (the user asked
for complete removal); raising the defaults (same failure, later); asking the
user to grant more budget on exhaustion (requires the user to stay present).

### Request usage view

The request account (`requestBudgets` in the dispatch record, keyed by accepted
human Turn/projection and binding epoch) holds an immutable task/attempt/nonce
per physical root account, registered at task startup inside existing rollback.
An attempt owing a deferred input translation binds at worker entry: after its
verdict, using exactly that settled source revision, when the translation had
already finished; otherwise to the authored revision, and that attempt then
records no verdict, so the source never moves under its account
(`docs/modules/ingest_media.md`, Non-blocking input translation). After a restart, an
interrupted attempt resumes as a new task/attempt under the unchanged durable
source and joins the same account; it never resets the old physical nonce.
The source Turn/projection, tenant and subject remain live fences: a restart
is not supersession, but a changed request or principal is refused.
A new attempt on the same human input joins the same account. A request whose
input is not genuine human input (a report relay, a channel event or any other
nonhuman initiator; `SyntheticSource`) opens no account and starts normally;
delegation from it is still refused unless the owner's own message reaches it
(below).

**The owner's message steered into a Turn without a request.** Problem: on
2026-11-12 (21:42–21:55) the Global Router applied the owner's standing
instruction while the tofu Project Router answered a commitment-due notice, so
the forward was steered into that wake (ROUTER.md R2: steer when busy). Its
model acted on it: ten `follow_up`s, a delegation and a cancel were each
refused "Synthetic authorization origin denied", with a recovery that said to
retry, because authority came only from the Turn's input. Any owner message
typed into a Coordinator chat while it answers a relay, a notice or a channel
event met the same refusal (the composer steers without asking, "Session-free
coordination"). Decision: when the input is not genuine human input and the
Turn is not a continuation, `source_for_task` takes the first owner message
steered into that executing reply that its model has received: a user-steer
record whose committed author `require_human_turn` admits (a Global Router
forward through `verify_forwarded_turn`), delivered to this attempt (the
executor's confirmed consumption) or to an earlier attempt of the Turn. The
source is `{conversationId, turnId: <reply>, projectionRevision: 1, steerId}`
(`$defs.source`): a steer's author is committed once and never changes. Grant
admission verifies the record and its author in full; each later admission of
the admitted grant reads only the reply's metadata, so a Worker's live gate
never reads the Coordinator's reply body. Such a request opens no request
account (an account covers its request from the first model call, which came
before the message), its Worker objectives quote the steered words, and the
Global Router forwards it only by `source_steer_id`. Without such a message the
refusal is `NonhumanRequest` (`code: nonhuman_request`, `execution_started:
false`), whose recovery says that repeating the call fails the same way,
unless the owner's delegation reaches the lead (below).

**Lead Turns act under the owner's delegation (owner decision 2026-11-18).**
Problem: wake, relay and notice Turns of every lead could only read Workers;
the owner's commitment wakes, restart notices and keepalives stalled on
`nonhuman_request` and waited for an owner sentence. Decision: under the
owner's full grant ([ROUTER.md R9](ROUTER.md), "The delegation reaches every
lead", scope `lead_authority`) `source_for_task(delegated=True)` takes, after the
Turn's own human input and a steered owner message, the delegation itself as
the source: `{conversationId, turnId: <the lead reply>, projectionRevision: 1,
ownerDelegation: <gate action id>}` (`$defs.source`). The callers that act on
Workers pass `delegated=True`: delegation and its birth
(`authorize_from_coordinator`), `worker_control`, session control, review,
cancel, `finish_project_request` and `request_user_decision`. Request accounts,
forwarding, standing instructions and the Watchdog's review switch keep needing
the owner's words. The gate records one audit action per lead Attempt before
any effect; a Worker's birth re-checks that record and the live grant, so a
revocation refuses at once; an admitted grant's live gate reads only the reply
Turn's metadata. Such a request opens no request account (as a steered one), and
its Worker objective links the reply and the audit record instead of quoting
owner words. Specification: `tests/router/test_lead_delegation.py`, and in
separate OS processes (`tests/chaos/test_lead_delegation_processes.py`) a
Project Router's real commitment-due wake that dispatches a Worker, whose
`integration_submit` from its isolated task workspace succeeds (Workers may
submit their own checkpoint, `workerDeniedTools`), and a revocation mid-Turn
that refuses the same Turn's next dispatch and `restart_server`.

**A Project Router restarts Tofu itself, one restart at a time (lead audit
2026-11-18, recommendation #3).** Problem: only the Global Router restarted, so
every Project Router's restart (merged fixes live only after one) waited on a
relay, although the owner gave his authority to the leads entirely
(「我的权限要全部下放给负责人们」). Decision: a Project Router calls `restart_server`
under the owner's full grant (`under_delegation: true` on its own judgment, or an
owner trigger) through the same `owner_delegation.restart_server` path as the
Global Router: the same machine pre-checks (HEAD's storage engine deployed or
certified; the server manager on this checkout's source generation, refreshed
first by the documented pre-step; the protected-step rule), one restart at a
time across every lead (a concurrent request is refused `restart_in_progress`
naming the holding lead; every other lead receives a project-message report
when it starts and when it ends), refused inside an owner-declared blackout
window (`restart_blackout`; the owner declares windows through the Global
Router's `owner_settings` with his own words), and one audit record per
request, refusals included, in the delegation history and `logs/lead-audit/`.
An enumerated grant does not reach Project Routers. Alternatives rejected: (b)
a `request_restart` tool asking the Global Router to restart (keeps the relay,
a second path, and is not what the owner asked); a process-local lock (the
execution host, its shards and the API host are separate processes). Full
record: [ROUTER.md R9](ROUTER.md), "Restart single flight, blackout windows and
readiness". Specifications: `tests/router/test_owner_delegation.py`, and in
separate OS processes `tests/chaos/test_lead_restart_processes.py` (a Project
Router restarts an isolated instance; a second lead's concurrent request is
refused with the holder named and told the outcome).

**A lead acts on another project's sessions under the delegation.** Under the
full grant a Project Router reads (`get_conversation_status`) and continues,
resumes, stops or re-models (`control_conversation` with `owner_delegation`) a
session of another of the owner's projects it is asked to help; without the
grant both stay refused ("Conversation belongs to another project"). The gate
records the action with the target project (`target.path`) and the target
project's lead receives one cross-lead report naming the audit record
([ROUTER.md R9](ROUTER.md), "Cross-project action"). Accepting and landing
another binding's assignment stays with that binding's lead.

Alternatives rejected: queuing the owner's message behind such a Turn (it
reaches the model only after that Turn ends, and the Global Router could no
longer forward owner messages steered into its relay replies); counting a steer
the model has not received (it would authorize work nobody read); reading the
reply body at every Worker admission (a multi-megabyte Coordinator reply on
every Worker model call); pinning a copy of the author into every grant (a
second copy of an immutable record). Specifications:
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_workers.py` (the nonhuman-wake cases),
`test_coordinator_session_control.py::test_a_nonhuman_wake_follows_up_its_workers_once_the_owner_message_reaches_it`
and, over real storage,
`tests/router/test_proactive_instruction_dispatch.py::test_a_forward_steered_into_a_commitment_wake_is_that_turns_request`.

**An owner message in an automatic continuation controls every Worker of its
project (2026-11-14).** Problem: an automatic continuation (the system-principal
Turn a Worker result wakes, bound to the grant of the request it continues) may
control only that request's Workers. An owner message that reached it changed
nothing, whether steered in while it ran or forwarded by the Global Router (R2
steers a forward into a busy Project Router): on 2026-11-14 01:08–01:11 parallel's
lead, in continuation 5f4f5020 of request c4bbe78a, received the Router's forward
of the owner's 「现在进展如何」 and twice failed to cancel stranded row 1be49eb0
of an earlier request ("Automatic continuation may control only its original
request workers"). A revoked row whose attempt was never stopped could then never
be closed, and every restart reported it (Tofu's FRONTIER-HARNESS-1 3d589822 too),
although the role guidance tells a lead to cancel obsolete Workers on every new
owner message. Decision (since narrowed: Coordinator Turns now control, review
and cancel every Worker of their binding without an owner message, "Worker
control is scoped by the Coordinator binding"; the steered-message rule below
still decides the request-scoped gate): `worker_control` keeps an automatic
continuation to its original request's Workers unless an owner message reached
that reply, judged by the predicate above (`_steered_owner_source`: a user-steer
record whose committed author `require_human_turn` admits, a Global Router forward
through `verify_forwarded_turn`, delivered to this attempt or an earlier attempt
of the Turn). Such a reply controls every Worker of its Coordinator binding, as a
Turn the owner's message started does; the owner, tenant and binding-epoch fences
still apply, so another Coordinator's or binding's Workers stay out of reach. Its
delegation is unchanged: new Workers join the request it continues, under that
request's policy and account. Without such a message, including a nonhuman steer,
a forward whose words are not the owner's, an owner message queued as the next
request and one its model has not received, it is refused as before. Alternatives
