is retained evidence, including after withdrawal; returning it is not permission
to start another executor. Runtime admission remains independently required.

These checks are not one atomic proof of current request disposition and
coordination epoch. Binding metadata, conversation availability, Stop and source
records are read outside the dispatch commit. `one_decision` deduplicates those
reads; it is not a transaction joining their authorities. An ordinary Worker
bind selects its assignment document only. A Coordinator wake instead selects
its exact source cohort, checks `continuation.ensure_open`, latest wake and
pinned predecessor, and fences the predecessor document when present. Neither
path exposes a supported reconciliation-only disposition selector. An unresolved
`pendingDecision` belongs to its exact request budget; it is not a global ban on
already-issued independent work, nor a decision a binder may consume. A
request-budget point update need not advance the project head: a future exact
request predicate would need its selected budget document fenced, not just a
head read. Also, admitted `ownerDelegation` validation checks the lead reply's
identity/owner but skips the live delegated-action grant; it cannot stand in
for a fresh live delegation check.

The existing segmented commit can fence selected dispatch documents and exact
owned Turn witnesses in its transaction. Turn witnesses check revision, current
Attempt and status; they do not witness coordination binding metadata or durable
Stop. Legacy record CAS likewise joins no external authority. Consequently an
additional application read, even immediately before or after bind, cannot
establish the requested atomic admission. No automatic-start consumer or unused
reconciliation API is added.

A legitimate extension would need an explicitly scoped admission contract whose
single selected-authority transaction validates the exact assignment/grant,
original request identity and current request disposition, binding epoch and
withdrawal witnesses together with the unstarted association write. Every writer
of those witnesses must participate in that same conflict boundary on each
supported backend; absent or unsupported evidence must refuse. It must preserve
already-born lifecycle behavior and unrelated requests. The alternative is a
Coordinator-only notice of passive evidence: no binding, executor creation,
owner-decision consumption or automatic dispatch from the notice. The
Coordinator would still act only through its existing authorized tools. Neither
extension is implemented here.

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_bind_boundary.py`
(normal exact bind, scoped denials, withdrawn authority and already-bound
replay), the repository lifecycle fixtures, and
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_continuation.py` (exact request finish,
owner-decision and independent running-Worker controls). These unit fixtures
use synthetic ports; they do not prove a cross-authority transaction or live
backend admission.

### Restart-paused Worker continuation

Problem: startup recovery skipped every assignment that already had an execution
association, while a background-completion wake created an ordinary, unbound turn.
The assignment therefore stayed `interrupted` on the old attempt, and its result,
delivery and Coordinator wake never arrived.

Decision: a restart-paused assignment resumes automatically through the existing
startup recovery pass and the ordinary Continue command, in its own conversation,
under the same grant. Each assignment that the pass leaves unresumed is logged
with its reason: a warning when a restart-paused lane's grant was revoked or its
deadline passed, info for an in-scope lane that had run (a grant without the
durable-Stop lifecycle), debug for plain filters and settled executions. A
Coordinator wake row (`coordinator_profile`) is a filter: its restart-paused wake
continues through `continuation.recover_coordinator`, so after the 2026-11-18
05:11 restart the 63 settled or already continued wakes that every pass logged at
info read as stranded work. A revoked restart-paused lane reports `grant_revoked`
before its verdict, since nothing can continue it; 97d5197b and cd5774da had
been warned at every boot as `restart_paused_turn_effect_unknown`, asking their
Coordinators to check calls before a continuation the grant refuses. One `skipped` counter used to hide all of these (after the
2026-11-06 restart, four of five interrupted lanes got no attempt and no line
said why). Verification: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_recovery_reasons.py`. A new assignment records one deterministic workspace task
identity (`project-worker:<assignmentId>`) in accepted config and its association;
an older assignment without that field inherits its interrupted attempt's actual
workspace task. Every physical attempt works under that identity (its work id),
so the existing isolated checkout, its uncommitted bytes,
checkpoint lineage and delivery receipt continue instead of being recreated.
Its task event stream likewise continues after the predecessor's durable cursor
([Task event delivery](TASK_EVENT_DELIVERY.md#reused-task-identities-continue-their-durable-stream)),
and the resumed attempt, as its Turn's current bound Attempt, takes over the task
result recovery left `interrupted` while the predecessor stays fenced
([Storage](STORAGE.md#restart-readiness-and-recovery)).

Coordinator wakes continue the same way. Problem: continuation treated a
restart-interrupted Coordinator wake as settled and, once its backoff ran out,
minted a new wake whose brief made the Coordinator re-read every Worker; after
the 2026-11-08 restart both Coordinators lost their interrupted round this way.
Decision: `continuation._proposal` hands a restart-paused previous wake back as
`existing`, so `dispatch_authorized` continues it in place through the same
restart continuation (`project-dispatch-resume:<assignment>:<attempt>`) under
its grant. A restart-paused human request Turn that has no wake yet proposes
`resume_source`, and `recover_coordinator` continues that Turn in place through
the ordinary resume command of the restart resume pass
([Run continuity](RUN_CONTINUITY.md#restart-paused-conversations-resume-by-themselves)),
idempotent with that pass. A request Turn continued in place (by either, or by
the owner's Continue) owns follow-through until its current Attempt settles:
previously its interrupted original Attempt counted as settled, so a wake could
race the continued Attempt. Once the continued Turn settles, Worker results wake
the request as before. Alternatives rejected: keeping the fresh wake and adding
the interrupted round to its brief (a second resume path that still re-reads the
Workers); leaving the request Turn to the restart resume pass alone (it depends
on quiesce records the stopping generation writes, which the restart deploying
this change does not). Verification:
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_continuation.py::test_a_restart_paused_coordinator_wake_continues_in_place_instead_of_a_new_wake`,
`::test_a_restart_paused_human_request_continues_in_place_instead_of_a_new_wake`
and `::test_a_request_turn_the_owner_continued_owns_follow_through_until_it_settles`.

#### Restart-paused wakes resume in place unless a later input superseded them

Problem: after the 2026-11-10 restart the parallel Coordinator's interrupted
wake was followed by the owner's queued input and its live reply; each recovery
pass retried `continue` on the old Turn, which `turn.attempt.create` refuses
while a later Turn of the lane is live, so the conflict recurred. Worker
recovery also always sent `continue`, which a Regenerate-only settlement refuses
at every boot. On 2026-11-11 (18:28) the parallel Router's request Turn
8c1d64fe, answering owner-forwarded input ca86e183, was interrupted after it had
posted a progress report on its lane. Recovery read the report as the lane tail
and never continued the Turn. The next Turn on the lane was another request's
wake, which `worker_control` limits to that request's Workers. The Router lost
the authority the owner's input had given it.
Decision: one rule for both restart resume owners, the pure
`restart_resume.lane_tail_verdict` (tail, later count, the first later
non-ingested input that superseded the Turn). A resume completes the Turn in
place and preserves every later Turn on both authorities (only regenerate
discards later Turns, on the owner's acknowledgement; see
[CONVERSATION_SYNC_V3.md](contracts/CONVERSATION_SYNC_V3.md)), so only a
superseding input stops it. Reports the Turn appended stay where they are.
`ProjectDispatchRepository.restart_resume` applies the rule with the
settlement's `continue`, else `checkpoint_resume`, option and anchor;
`restart_paused` is true only for that resumable Turn, and
`ProjectDispatchService.dispatch_authorized` refuses any other restart-paused
Turn with `RestartContinuationRefused`. Worker recovery leaves such assignments
unresumed with a typed reason (`restart_paused_turn_superseded_by:<turn>`,
`restart_paused_turn_offers_no_continue`, and, as a warning,
`restart_paused_turn_effect_unknown` for a Turn whose settlement names calls the
restart cut off with an unknown side effect: its Coordinator's `poll` row names
them (`error.unknownEffects`), and the Coordinator checks them, then continues
it with `control_conversation`; see
[Turn settlement](contracts/TURN_SETTLEMENT.md)). A wake or request Turn the
owner's newer input superseded, or whose settlement names such calls, settles
once: the request continues in one new wake at the lane tail, born through
ordinary admission and deferred while the lane is busy, whose brief records the
interrupted Turn and why (what superseded it, or the calls to check first);
that wake is the request's next `previous`, so the old Turn is never proposed
again.
Nothing is written to a Turn or Attempt that is not resumed, and no record needs
a backfill. Alternatives rejected: revoking the stale wake's grant (it silences
the request, including one displaced only by another request's wake at boot);
continuing a reported-on Turn in a new wake at the tail (it becomes a synthetic
continuation scoped to its own request's Workers, and the interrupted round is
lost); resuming a Turn a later input superseded (the newer input owns the lane).

The verdict reads only the Turns after the interrupted one, and so do the
decision-reply check (`continuation._human_replied`) and the one-time decision
of an owner question indexed before answers were recorded
(`lib/router/proactive/questions.py`, docs/ROUTER.md R10). Problem:
on 2026-11-12 these reads asked `list_turns` for the Turns after one ordinal,
and `list_turns` read the whole lane with `turn.list` before filtering it. The
Tofu Project Router's lane had grown past the authority's response bound, so
after the 15:50 restart every wake pass was refused ("turn list exceeds its
bounded streamed payload", about 1,900 times by 17:09) and answered owner
questions stayed open. Decision: `list_turns(after_ordinal=...)` requires its
`lane_id` and reads that suffix newest first, one `turn.sync.page` per Turn,
stopping at the Turn right after the ordinal; the callers pass their Turn's
lane. Alternatives rejected: a separate reader per caller (the three callers and
their test seams keep the `list_turns` shape); an ordinal window on `turn.list`
(an engine contract change and a new certified build). Verification:
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_wake_bounded_reads.py`.

One failing wake never stops the pass. `recover_coordinator` handles every
`_proposal` shape explicitly (`existing`, `resume_source`, `wait_until`, a new
wake; anything else is a typed fault), binds the proposal per source, and
attributes a failure to the Coordinator named by that request's own source row
and to the canonical project, with its wake identity. Settlement drain faults
likewise name `canonicalProjectPath`, never a Worker checkout. Verification:
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_restart_recovery.py`.

Durable-worker attempts. Problem: `turn.attempt.dispatch_worker` binds every
Attempt to its own task `conversation-attempt:<attemptId>`, while task
registration adopted the accepted config's workspace task id as the physical
id. The two never match, so after the durable admission cutover every
Coordinator continuation and Worker failed at registration (`prepared task
identity differs from conversation worker`), and no failed or restart-paused
Worker could continue. Decision: the physical task and the work id are separate
identities. A worker claim alone names the physical task, which owns the task
result, event stream and Turn binding of that one attempt; only an unclaimed
start adopts the captured id as its task. The work id is the association's
`taskId`: `bind_account` stamps it on every physical attempt
(`workspace_delivery.workspace_work_id`). The checkout registration, binding,
delivery settlement, publication, review, shared-file candidates and the
Coordinator's integration tools all key by it, and the occurrence ledger uses it
as its single root. A successor therefore binds the predecessor's checkout and
resumes the model, tool and reservation sequences instead of colliding with
them. That holds whether the predecessor was interrupted by a restart or failed
before entry. The workspace origin records the physical attempt in it as
`executorTaskId`, and delivery recovery reads that attempt's verdict while
settling the work id. Alternatives rejected:
- Keeping the work id as the physical task: storage fences worker writes by the
  `conversation-attempt:` root and enqueues one job per Attempt.
- Executing project dispatch in the API process again: this reverses the durable
  execution admission decision in [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md).
- Refusing such successors: no Worker could continue after any failure or
  restart.
- Re-keying the checkout to each new task: this needs a storage handoff
  operation and moves Git refs on every attempt.

The view's `execution.taskId` stays the attempt's physical task, and
`delivery.taskId` names the work id. Restoring ordinals still needs an uncapped
tool account, as before.

Specs:
- `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_file_lifecycle.py::test_successor_continues_the_assignment_checkout_as_its_own_task`
- `tests/conversation/test_durable_attempt_execution.py::test_accepted_workspace_task_id_never_renames_the_claimed_task`
- `tests/tasks_pkg/test_conversation_worker_binding.py::test_project_grant_workspace_id_never_renames_the_claimed_task`
- `tests/project_mod/test_delivery_resume_demand.py::test_working_recovery_reads_the_executing_attempt_and_settles_the_work_id`
- `tests/conversation/test_worker_attempt_real_start.py`: a fresh Worker, a
  restart-paused Worker resumed by boot recovery, and Coordinator and owner
  Continues of a failed Worker Turn, on SQLite and native storage. Delegation,
  the durable claim, task registration, `bind_account` and the Git checkout are
  production code; only the model context and the run loop's provider outcome
  are controlled. The identity cases fail at `917c6390e3`.

A trusted continuation revalidates the live grant, requires an exact settled,
unreviewed predecessor and its accepted successor, then advances the association
to that successor. The accepted `_resumeSource` of every successor of the bound
Turn (Continue, checkpoint resume, guidance answer or Regenerate) also restores
the assignment's conserved model and tool accounts, whatever settled its
predecessor: the last model ordinal, cumulative usage and original nonce, the
last tool-occurrence ordinal and the shared-request tool-reservation counter
continue once, never restart at one or fabricate another occurrence account. An
account with no recorded occurrence has nothing to continue and starts like a
first attempt.
Problem: `bind_account` proposed a zero reservation counter for every generation
and refused any stored counter that differed, so a resumed Worker that had
reserved a tool failed to start (`Worker tool reservations cannot be
replenished`); with only that changed, its first tool still collided with stored
tool-occurrence ordinal one. Restricting recovery to `server_restart` also made
later retries after a failed start hit the same refusal. Decision: verify the
accepted receipt's exact predecessor attempt, task, Turn, conversation and settled
status. A successor whose stored counter names the same work id and nonce keeps
that counter, and `ExecutionBudget.restore_tool_account` continues both tool
sequences without restoring spent slots (a finite tool cap refuses, as for the
model account). A fresh start, another root or nonce, a live predecessor, or
evidence that differs from the accepted receipt still refuses. Alternatives
rejected: resetting the counter
erases the request's recorded tool usage and is the replenishment the fence
exists to stop; a fresh nonce per generation forks the one durable account; and
accepting reservation ordinals beyond `count + 1` would hide lost or forged
reservations; adopting the stored count only as an offset over budget ordinals
that restart at one still collides the first resumed tool with occurrence ordinal
one, and stacking that offset on the restore counts every stored reservation twice.
Specs: `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_request_budget.py::test_accepted_resume_continues_the_worker_tool_reservation_counter`,
`::test_resumed_worker_cannot_replenish_or_adopt_another_tool_reservation_counter`,
`::test_resume_receipt_must_match_exact_predecessor_before_restoring_counts`,
`tests/storage/test_coordinator_resume_allowance.py` (all selected authorities),
`tests/agent_core/test_tool_account_restore.py` and
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_workers.py::test_restart_continue_keeps_counting_the_worker_tool_reservations`,
`::test_a_new_physical_account_still_cannot_replenish_worker_tool_reservations`
and `::test_each_restart_generation_continues_the_one_worker_tool_account_once`.

Problem (2026-11-07): only a restart Continue kept the account, so a successor
whose immediate predecessor ended any other way could not start once the Worker
had called a tool. The common chain was a restart interrupting the Worker, its
boot-recovery Continue failing before entry, and a Coordinator
`control_conversation` continue of that failed attempt refused in 3 s with
`Worker tool reservations cannot be replenished` (three lanes at 08:57; sixteen
lanes on 11-05 and 11-06 after a provider failure or a failed restart Continue).
The owner's browser Continue escaped the refusal only because it ran unbound,
outside the grant. Every such lane had to be re-delegated. Decision: `bind_account`
continues the one account for every successor whose `_resumeSource` names a
settled (not pending or running) attempt of the bound Turn in the recipient
conversation: the same work-id root and nonce, the stored counter and restored
ordinals. A fresh account (no resume source), another root or nonce, a live
predecessor and a predecessor from another Turn still refuse. Continuing is not
replenishment: the counter and ordinals only grow, and the Turn authority and the
association's review fence still decide whether a successor may exist at all.
Alternatives rejected:
- Keeping the restart-only rule: any platform failure after a Worker's first tool
  call ends its lane, and recovery means a new lane, checkout and context.
- Allowing selected predecessor causes: a cause allow-list grows with every new
  failure code, and why the predecessor ended does not change the account.
- Walking back to the last restart-interrupted ancestor: provider failures and
  Stops still refuse, and the account lives on the assignment, not the lineage.
- A fresh account per successor: it resets the counter or forks the nonce, and
  its first model or tool occurrence collides with stored ordinal one.

Specs: `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_request_budget.py::test_every_successor_of_the_bound_turn_continues_the_worker_tool_account`,
`::test_resumed_worker_cannot_replenish_or_adopt_another_tool_reservation_counter`
and, through the real start path,
`tests/conversation/test_worker_attempt_real_start.py::test_a_coordinator_continue_of_a_failed_worker_turn_starts_and_the_assignment_follows`.

A background-command completion for an eligible unfinished execution uses this
same Continue path; its receipts stay durable and are replayed into the resumed
Worker turn. The
ordinary settlement hook now observes the successor's status, result and delivery,
and wakes the Coordinator. A completed/reviewed execution never resumes; exact
command replay is read-only. Late background evidence for a completed or reviewed
execution attaches once to its exact settled Turn with a CAS fence, then
acknowledges the inbox receipt; it neither starts a model nor changes the review.
Problem: treating that evidence as Continue caused a permanent `database_conflict`
retry loop because the settled execution correctly disallowed continuation.
Decision: use the existing closed-execution evidence settlement path, including
revoked assignments. Alternative rejected: allowing Continue on a reviewed or
completed assignment would reopen accepted work. Stop, a live predecessor,
changed source or lost
workspace/lineage evidence refuses recovery rather than starting a second writer.

Problem: a Continue whose executor refused to start (for example `Worker tool
reservations cannot be replenished`) settles the Turn `failed` with Regenerate as
its only resume option, so the Turn authority refused every later batch Continue and
the maintenance tick re-issued it, failing with `database_conflict`, forever.
Decision: a bound batch resumes only through the Continue its execution Turn offers.
A settled execution Turn without that option is closed: the batch settles once as
evidence on it through the same path, logging `cause=execution_not_continuable`
with the Turn status and settlement cause; the failed settlement stays unchanged.
A batch whose own deterministic Continue attempt has already settled is not
replayed; its receipts wait for the next explicit turn. Unbound receipts keep the
ordinary wake. Alternatives rejected: Regenerate discards the Worker's transcript
and checkpoint; classifying refusals by retry count or error text adds a knob and
still re-issues the refused command; a fresh queued turn has no exact
predecessor/successor association. Specs:
`tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_workers.py::test_worker_execution_without_continue_settles_its_background_batch_once`
and `::test_background_batch_issues_its_continue_once`.

`blocked: task_background_commands_running` means that durable publication was
captured while the Worker's background commands were still running; it is not a
successful delivery and does not prove those commands survived the old server.
Startup command recovery publishes finished results or an explicit died-with-server
result. The collective completion resumes the same assignment and clears the hold
through the existing delivery retry/settlement owner once its exact task and
background work are no longer running. There is no storage rewrite, new poller,
fresh delegated Worker or replacement worktree. Alternatives rejected: a new
Worker rebuilt from the old transcript loses execution context and can put two
Workers on one checkout; binding only a later turn by conversation ID lets an
unrelated turn steal the assignment; clearing `blocked` at boot can publish an
unfinished checkpoint.

Problem: a completion that arrived after `cancel_project_work` still fell through
to the ordinary collective wake, creating a tool-capable turn whose result was not
linked to the cancelled assignment. The same wake could already be leased when the
grant was withdrawn.

Decision: the existing background-batch owner checks the frozen assignment binding
before either Continue or the collective wake turn. When its exact grant is revoked
and its bound Turn and Attempt have settled, it attaches the complete durable command
receipts as one deterministic `_bgCommandInjects` evidence block on that cancelled
Turn and starts no wake. No model attempt, replacement Worker or new evidence store
is created, and no still-running command is killed. Replay of the same block is
idempotent, and a wake row stored by an earlier release is retired without a turn.
A revoked grant whose execution is still settling, lacks evidence, or loses the
projection race also starts no model turn; its durable receipts remain available
for a later evidence-settlement pass. Because those receipts stay queued, every
maintenance tick revisits them. Once a settlement's block is attached (or it
had none to attach), the process remembers it with every input of its verdict
(`background_command_delivery._CLOSED_SETTLEMENTS`): the receipt set, the
assignment's document version (`assignment_version`; none for a revocation or
review, which never change again) and the Turn's status and `projectionRevision`.
While those stand, a tick reads only that version and the Turn's metadata, so
the verdict and the attachment cannot differ; any of them changing decides
again, and a Turn revision alone still proves the block attached without
rereading a multi-megabyte projection. On 2026-11-12 the ticks re-decided 64
closed Workers every minute (assignment read three times, grant validated live:
22-26% of the execution host's dispatch reads). The maintenance drain forgets
conversations that no longer hold receipts. Exact assignment reads expose the evidence
through the bound result. A human entering
the Worker conversation still uses the ordinary human-authorized command path;
this is not a user Stop and never reports an authority refusal as one.

Alternatives rejected: killing background commands at cancellation discards
useful finished evidence and adds a second command-lifecycle owner; relying only
on `TrustedDispatch.checked()` raises a revocation error that the legacy ordinary
wake path would otherwise bypass; deleting the completion queue rows would lose
evidence; appending a settled user Turn would fabricate human/model activity and
leave the assignment unbound. Ordinary non-Worker completions retain their
existing collective model wake, and restart-paused assignments continue through
the same Continue path described above.

Problem: a Worker turn that ran without its assignment binding (started by an
older wake path, or by human input in the Worker conversation) leaves receipts
with no binding. Their completions took the ordinary collective wake after
cancellation, and each wake turn's own receipts were unbound again, so the Worker
conversation kept working. Cancellation reached only the attempt named by the
execution association, which can be long settled while such a turn runs.

Decision: a Worker conversation (created with `_coordinatorSource`) is governed by
its assignment. A background batch whose receipts carry no binding resolves the
conversation's latest recipient admission through its canonical project
(`recovery.worker_conversation_admission`) and follows the revoked-grant settlement
above; a live grant keeps the existing wake. Revocation through
`cancel_project_work` or the assignment revoke API stops the associated attempt and
every other live attempt in the Worker conversation, with the same
`dispatch_grant_revoked` cause and provenance. A Coordinator continuation grant
names the Coordinator conversation as its recipient, so its turns are never swept.
Human input in a cancelled Worker conversation still runs on the ordinary command
path; its command results also settle as durable evidence instead of waking a
model turn.

Alternatives rejected: recording a durable conversation Stop for the Worker
reports an authority withdrawal as a user Stop and still admits wakes requested
after it; refusing every unbound automatic turn in Worker conversations would also
block steering and human takeover of live Workers; rebinding an unbound wake into
a trusted Continue fails on the stale association and would retroactively give
that chain the assignment's authority.

The existing command/start handshake owns execution, lane-idle admission and
queued-human priority. Busy/superseded dispatch is deferred. After the existing
human/background queues drain, the lane-settlement callback retries the same grant;
startup discovers persisted owner/project records through existing cursor pagination.
Only new `lifecycleVersion=1` grants auto-retry; legacy evidence remains readable,
not automatically upgraded. Exact-command retry cannot buy a second execution.

`ExecutionBudget` remains reservation/settlement authority. Its physical-attempt
observer persists `dispatch_intent/settled/voided`, root/attempt task IDs, ordinal,
reserved/reported tokens, usage completeness and an account nonce. Retries share
the grant caps. Unresolved intent, incomplete paid usage or former-task usage
blocks new spending; restart/account recreation does not replenish authority.
Every settlement must retain its nonce and occurrence identity; even an unsent
settlement cannot void durable uncertain intent. Live authority is revalidated
before physical admission; this is not atomic network revocation or provider
exactly-once. Consultation still exposes no tools. An explicit `files` profile
names only first-party `read_files/write_file/edit_file`, relative read/write
prefixes and a tool cap. Write prefixes must permit preimage reads; `.` is an
explicit whole-project prefix, never a directory scan or metadata exemption.
Tool admission and nonce-bound durable occurrences conserve the same authority.
Shell, recursive indexes, PTC, MCP, remote/ref adapters, plugins, children and
paid auxiliary translation stay closed. `autoTranslate` is forced false.
Auxiliary compaction summaries charge the grant's own bound account under its
recorded ceilings ([CONTEXT_COMPACTION.md](CONTEXT_COMPACTION.md#auxiliary-summaries-under-dispatch-grants)).
Batch admission judges only calls that can execute: a call already rejected by
parse or its contract returns that rejection, and a file-scope argument-shape
refusal (for example `read_files` with both `path` and `reads`) is a contract
rejection of that call only; authority refusals still fail the turn. Decision:
`docs/contracts/TURN_SETTLEMENT.md`; specification:
`test_project_dispatch_tool_contract_rejection.py`.

Problem: a tool name/path string alone can borrow a plugin, alias or canonical
checkout. Decision: pin the exact first-party handler, bind the physical task's
registered isolated workspace, and use anchored directory descriptors, no-follow
regular-file I/O, byte-exact preimages, durable undo and cooperative target leases.
Ordinary tasks default to direct execution, but dispatched file workers always
retain isolation, including with `TOFU_WORKSPACE_MODE=direct` or local
`tofu.workspaceMode=direct`; those settings cannot bypass review/publication.
Recheck live authority and workspace ownership at I/O/publication. Reads consume
complete UTF-8 text; writes and exact preimage capture have no private byte quota.
Every explicit read/edit batch entry is admitted by its path/tool grant, not a
20/30-entry ceiling. Explicit human call allowances remain authoritative.
Runtime, Git, credential and cache paths are refused even under `.`. Alternatives
rejected: opaque programs and generic converters cannot be safely path-fenced.
This is not an OS sandbox against privileged external reparenting after the final
ancestry check; those writers remain outside cooperative containment.

Revocation uses grant-revision CAS and cancels the bound existing attempt; a
repeat retries the same cancellation after ACK loss. Human Stop remains the
existing turn/attempt cancellation authority, not natural-language steering.
Neither late reports nor read/recovery reactivate stopped or revoked work.
Review requires the current completed result revision (the Worker Turn's
`modelContextRevision`, never advanced by display-only enrichment) and records immutable
`accept/rework/reject` evidence; it does not change task completion or grant
revision. `reworkOf` links the exact old grant/result and immutable rework review;
execution requires a fresh human source, command, model and budget, not renewed
old authority. Stop entry points durably withdraw existing referenced grants;
TTL expiry, late reports and boot/idle retry cannot restore withdrawn authority.

Stop also stores one monotonic owner/conversation watermark in the declared
`stopNamespace`, independent of current project metadata. Admission checks two
point reads; this prevents restoring an old project from restoring authority.
Human attempt/queued/task/registry-miss Stop paths record it; exact-grant internal
cancellation does not withdraw peer grants. Storage failure is not a durable-Stop ACK.
Stop skips the repository lock when no grant created before the Stop references
that conversation. Otherwise it tries the lock without waiting and, if acquired,
rereads the grants under the same lock that orders publication's final checks.

Problem: a Stop signals the executor first, yet grant-record revocation could
wait on integration's repository lock or fail at its bounded CAS. Decision: the
acknowledged watermark is the withdrawal, because admission and every live executor
fence refuse each grant created before it. If the repository lock is already held
or revocation encounters `DispatchContention` (bounded CAS attempts ended in
conflicts or uncertain ACKs), Stop reports `dispatchWithdrawal: pending_revocation`
instead of `complete` without waiting or attempting the remaining revocations.
Lane-settlement or startup recovery revokes, from the same durable watermark, an
unstarted grant it would otherwise retry. Admission already fences every other
referenced grant, including a started executor's; a repeated Stop also revokes its
record. An unacknowledged watermark or any other lock, storage, integrity or
metadata failure still fails the Stop; the executor stays signalled and its idle
projection is still published, and repeating the same Stop is idempotent.
Alternatives rejected:
waiting for publication to release the lock (does not change withdrawal),
catching every revocation failure (hides storage and configuration faults), retrying
beyond the CAS budget or adding a revocation queue or poller (unbounded work), and
writing the watermark before the executor signal (delays every Stop behind dispatch
storage). Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_recovery.py`.

Problem: automatic completed-file delivery would precede review. Decision: stamp
the assignment admission into initial workspace registration and hold all
initial/no-change/recovery/postgate publication paths. Acceptance remains review
only. Explicit `/publish` pins grant, result and `expectedCheckpointSha`; existing
Git gates and `origin.delivery` own publication. Final fresh checks run under the
same repository lock used by grant revocation, before intent and apply/fast-forward.
Changing the checkpoint cannot reuse an old publish request. No new delivery store
or worker is introduced. `canonicalDelivery=true` derives only from the exact
owner/project/task-matching durable `delivered` receipt with commit evidence
(`delivery.canonical_delivery`), not completion, acceptance, candidate `merged` or a
publish request; an uncommitted working-tree publication projects as `pending` or
`blocked` (`delivered_uncommitted`).
Alternatives rejected: acceptance-as-publication and UI-only fencing lose authority.

### Dispatch storage waits

Problem: a transient storage `deadline_elapsed` while validating a grant or
recording a physical occurrence stopped a healthy Worker as
`dispatch_authority_lost`, even when the authority recovered moments later.
Decision: live dispatch validation and its model/tool occurrence observers use
the shared `lib/tasks_pkg/persistence_wait.py` lifecycle. Typed transient storage
failures wait with the existing backoff and liveness witness, without an
agent-attempt ceiling. Only the read or idempotent occurrence mutation repeats;
no model request, tool effect, grant or execution account is recreated.
Repository contention errors retain the final storage cause, so their bounded
CAS loop cannot erase the distinction between transient pressure and a permanent
refusal. Malformed config is refused before a workspace ownership lookup.
Validation rereads live authority on recovery, and new intents revalidate after
their durable ACK before allowing effects. Stop cancels admission waits; already
observed model/tool settlement continues to ACK after Stop. Shutdown retains the
existing persistence-debt failure behavior. Permanent integrity/permission
failures and real revocations still fail closed with their original causes.
An uncertain ACK reuses the exact occurrence; an existing identical finite-grant
intent consumes no second allowance.

Alternatives rejected: ignoring a failed authority check could execute without
authorization; retrying the whole turn could repeat paid or external effects;
another retry count/deadline would kill work during longer storage outages.
Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_persistence.py`,
`tests/storage/test_project_dispatch_persistence.py` and the existing dispatch
runtime, occurrence accounting and stop-cause contracts.

### Dispatch contention is not an authority verdict

Problem: every dispatch write commits through one head per owner/project: each
Worker and Coordinator model or tool occurrence, reservation, delegation,
binding, review and revocation. Every segmented snapshot read is fenced by the
same head. A write retries its CAS `casAttempts` times back to back and then
raises `DispatchContention`; a read raises it when the head advanced under each
snapshot. The storage waits above classified only transient storage codes. So a
Worker whose own model settlement lost to concurrent writers (a Coordinator
burst, or the herd a storage stall releases at once) stopped as
`dispatch_authority_lost`. The same verdict failed a Worker's tool intent as a
tool error, ended a Coordinator request as `ModelAdmissionRefused`, and reached
the Coordinator as `get_project_workers` "retry reading".

Decision: contention proves only that another writer committed.
`repository.transient_dispatch_failure` classifies it by the nearest dispatch
verdict in the cause chain: `DispatchContention` is transient, and every other
`ProjectDispatchError` (revocation, a changed source or binding, malformed
evidence) is final. Without one, the transient storage codes decide.
`admission.wait_for_dispatch` runs the persistence wait with that classifier.
Sites that never had a persistence fence use `admission.reread_on_contention`:
the first attempt runs as before, so an uncontended call keeps its existing
Stop handling, and only a transient failure enters the wait. Together they
cover every dispatch call site that runs inside a task:
- Worker validation (`validated_dispatch`), its account read and reservation
  counter, and its model, tool and reservation occurrences;
- the Coordinator request account (`bind_source_account`): source read, account
  registration, dispatch admission, occurrences and tool reservations;
- the Coordinator reads `get_project_workers`, `get_project_assignment`, the
  polls and snapshots of a Worker `poll`, and the pre-dispatch read of
  `dispatch_project_assignment`;
- the Worker birth of `delegate_project_work`, whose documented recovery is to
  replay unchanged arguments.
Each repeat reads and validates the live grant again. A revocation or Stop that
committed meanwhile is met on the next read and stops at once with its own
cause.

Bound: the persistence-wait liveness contract, not a count: ACK, owner Stop
(reads and intents) or server shutdown (settlement debt returns to the recovery
owner). Its existing backoff spreads a herd's retries, and every CAS round has
a winner, so a burst drains. No retry count, interval or timeout was added.

Alternatives rejected:
- raising `casAttempts` or adding jitter: a tuned knob, and a longer burst still
  kills;
- per-assignment heads: see "Point updates commit their own documents" below,
  which removed the shared head from point updates instead;
- re-running a whole Worker wait: it would lose a settled Worker's
  progress result.

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_contention.py`. It
races Coordinator delegations and foreign commits against a Worker's model and
tool occurrences, tears authority snapshots, revokes and Stops during
contention, and covers the Coordinator request account, delegation and read
tools. The Coordinator write tools are covered by the next section. Not
covered: the dispatch itself of `dispatch_project_assignment` still returns
contention as an uncertain outcome with its existing recovery. Recovery passes
outside tasks are covered by "Durable Coordinator wakes".

### Point updates commit their own documents, never the project head

Problem: every dispatch write committed through the one head CAS of its
owner/project, and every index walk is valid only while that head is
unchanged. About 14 live Workers advanced the head with every model and tool
occurrence and reservation, so in about 25 hours to 2026-11-11 the execution
host's `project.dispatch.commit` failed about 123,000 times against 66,000
successes (`tofu_execution_storage_rpc_seconds`), and any read or write that
needed the head unchanged across several storage calls (a delegation's cohort
reads, a review) waited for 15 minutes or more on `Dispatch snapshot advanced`,
presented as "Waiting for storage". Process-local combining and a per-head FIFO
lane only reordered that contention inside one process and added locks.

Decision: the head changes only when evidence is created. `segmented.commit`
sends a *document commit* (`project.dispatch.commit` without `expected_revision`
and `head`, `contracts/project_dispatch_storage_v2.json`) when a delta creates
no document, index or count and its snapshot walked no index: each written
document's own `expected_version` fences it, atomically across the commit, and
the head is neither read nor written. Worker model, tool and reservation
occurrences, request-account occurrences, association binds and
continuations, reviews, revocations and publications are document commits.
Creations (grants, batches, settlements, scope changes, request accounts) and
any update decided from an index walk (a `mutate` aggregate `check`, a cohort
or full read) commit through the head CAS, so they still fence each other and
the walks they read. Consequences:

- Writers of different rows never conflict; writers of one row conflict only
  on that document's CAS and re-read it, as before.
- An index walk is torn only by a creation, never by occurrences.
- `mutate` without a `check` reads only its own row (`_point_selection`): no
  policy rows, no prerequisites (`dependencies.check_graph(selected=True)`
  skips prerequisites a stored selection omits; grants are still checked
  complete), so an occurrence costs one head read, one document read and one
  commit.
- A commit no longer re-reads the head after its base: the CAS it commits
  through verifies the base.
- A read-only document is not fenced: an aggregate decision over rows it
  does not write sees a concurrent point update of them only on its next read.
  The point updates move each row forward (bound, reviewed, revoked, settled),
  so an aggregate verdict read before one stays conservative.

No lock, lane, queue, count, window or timeout exists; the only retry is the
existing bounded CAS re-read inside the persistence wait.

Alternatives rejected:
- combining concurrent point updates into one head commit, then a per-head
  FIFO lane with exclusive turns for writes and torn re-reads (both shipped on
  2026-11-11 and removed): process-local locks that serialized every writer of
  a project and still lost to other processes;
- per-assignment heads: the same structural gain with a second witness per
  row to maintain;
- read-set fences (assert the version of every read document at commit): a
  new storage step for a write-skew case no current verdict depends on.

Measured on SQLite and the source-matched native engine
(`tests/storage/test_project_dispatch_repository_segments.py`): eight
concurrent point updates of different rows make eight document commits, no
head commit and no lost CAS, and the head is unchanged; an index walk during
which a Worker records an occurrence for every row it reads completes with one
opening head read and one witness. Storage contract:
`tests/storage/test_project_dispatch_segments.py` (receipts, replay, per-document
conflict rollback, racing rows, refused creations/indexes/deletes/partial heads).

### A delegation reads each fact once and never waits on another row's writer

Problem (2026-11-11, Coordinator `coordinator-1a390889`): four concurrent
`delegate_project_work` calls took 1,713, 1,862, 2,034 and 2,735 s, each re-run
up to 16 times by its persistence wait after `Dispatch snapshot advanced`.
Every segmented read re-read the project head after its documents, and the
about 14 live Workers advance that head with each model and tool occurrence, so
a multi-call read on the overloaded storage host rarely survived eight times in
a row, and each re-run repeated the whole delegation. One delegation made about
405 storage calls and grew with its request: `request_delegation` walked the
request's budget cohort six times to find its first Worker, and a Worker birth
validated its grant about eleven times (`TrustedDispatch` for its principal,
birth time, work id and each bind, plus one nested in the bind CAS), each
validation reading the binding three times and the Coordinator's Stop twice.

Decision:
- Point reads carry no head witness: each document is read atomically and
  never removed, so a selection without index walks is consistent per document
  (`segmented.load`). Index/count walks still re-read the head, and a commit's
  own CAS verifies its base, so no torn snapshot commits.
- The request's first Worker comes from the head of its budget index
  (`first_of_budgets`: one index row and one document) and the Coordinator task
  keeps it, since its delegation never changes. The pre-conversation check and
  the grant CAS select that row, the budget and the source settlement instead
  of the source and budget cohorts: every grant was compared with the earlier
  ones in this CAS, so the first stands for the cohort.
- A Coordinator wake's grant CAS also selects the source cohort. Its check
  re-derives the wake from the request's Workers, as `recover_coordinator`
  derived it, and the first row alone lists fewer of them. Problem: from the
  2026-11-12 15:50 restart, when that check started comparing whole wakes,
  every new wake of a multi-Worker request was retained as
  `WakeEvidenceChanged` (1,629 times by 21:36, on all four coordinators), so
  Worker results woke no Coordinator. Spec:
  `tests/storage/test_coordinator_wake_admission.py` (segmented SQLite and
  native; the portable v1 fixture reads the whole record and cannot show it).
- One live decision reads each external authority record once
  (`repository.one_decision`, `decision_read`: binding, Stop watermark,
  conversation metadata, Turn, attempt, request budget). The decisions are the
  delegation's check before its Worker conversation exists, the grant
  admission with its CAS attempts, and each own-authority validation. Separate
  fences never share a decision.
- A Worker birth validates once per effect fence: before its Turn exists (the
  dispatch decision, handed to `capture` by `TrustedDispatch.admitted`), where
  the Turn's association binds (outside the bind CAS, which fences revocation,
  revision and the still-unbound association), and where the accepted attempt
  is admitted to a worker. The principal, birth time and work id reuse the
  command's latest validation. An existing association stays evidence returned
  without validation.
- The association bind is a document commit (previous section); the
  Coordinator grant is a creation through the head CAS.

Alternatives rejected:
- more `casAttempts` or longer backoff: knobs, and the head advances for as
  long as Workers run;
- keeping validations across commands or for a whole delegation: a Stop,
  revocation or rebinding between fences would go unseen;
- one validation per birth: creating the Turn is an effect, so its bind must
  see a Stop that landed meanwhile.

Measured on the SQLite authority through the real command service, one
request: the first delegation went from 405 to 124 storage calls, later ones
from 423-439 (growing) to 116 (constant), four concurrent delegations from
2,224 to 581. Specifications:
`test_point_work_survives_other_rows_creations_and_creations_still_fence` and
`test_a_worker_birth_binds_beside_concurrent_occurrences_without_conflict`
(`tests/storage/test_project_dispatch_repository_segments.py`),
`tests/storage/test_coordinator_delegation_reads.py`, and the read budgets of
`tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_admission_once.py`.

### Delegation returns at once; its Worker is born on its own thread

Problem (owner decision 2026-11-11: speed comes first, stability is restored
later; a human starts a conversation in an instant, and delegating is the same
act): even with the reads above, `delegate_project_work` held the Coordinator
for the whole Worker birth (about 116 storage calls, Git preflight, root
preparation, the conversation, the grant CAS, the Turn and the dispatch), tens
to hundreds of seconds per call on the loaded host, one call after another.
Worker start after dispatch (87 production births, 2026-11-11 logs): workspace
binding p50 22 s, p90 68 s, max 159 s, of which the isolation preflight was
p50 4.6 s, p90 17 s and the two-walk input snapshot p50 6.7 s, p90 18 s.

Decision:
- A call without attachments admits from memory (`delegation.admit_in_memory`:
  the active Coordinator request, synthetic input, read-only roots, the issued
  policy and model). A call with attachments also preflights the durable source
  before retaining files (see [Attachments on lead messages](#attachments-on-lead-messages)).
  The call returns the deterministic assignment and conversation IDs with
  `status: starting`. The birth (`coordinator_workers._born`: one-decision
  validation, root preparation, conversation, grant, Turn, dispatch) runs on
  its own thread, recorded on the Coordinator task (`coordinator_births`). The
  key's origin attempt comes from the task (`delegation.origin_attempt`: the
  request account's first attempt, or the continuation's grant); the birth
  recomputes it from storage and refuses a mismatch.
- Every later Coordinator tool sees births. A Worker `poll` first
  waits for the selected births under its own deadline, Stop and input
  (`starting` rows when its time runs out) and returns a refused birth as
  `failed_to_start` with the refusal's code, details and recovery;
  `get_project_workers` lists births storage does not show yet under
  `starting_workers`; `cancel_project_work` and `finish_project_request` wait
  for births in flight. A replay joins a birth in flight or runs a finished one
  again (reusing the accepted Worker, re-dispatching a deferred grant, retrying
  a refusal); different arguments for one in flight are `CommandReused`.
- A birth may complete after the attempt that issued it completed
  (`source_for_task(issued=True)`); a Stop, failure or supersession still
  refuses it. A restart ends births with their task: an await then reports a
  delegation without a grant as `failed_to_start`, whose recovery replays it.
- A Worker conversation's header is written outright: `conversation.create`
  refuses an existing ID and replays its own command's receipt, so only that
  refusal reads the existing conversation. The Coordinator's folder is read
  once per task.
- The Coordinator's tool reservations only count. One flush at a time writes
  the newest count off the tool's path, reservations arriving meanwhile fold
  into the next flush, and none revalidates the request: every Coordinator
  tool checks its own authority. A model dispatch keeps one live validation
  before its intent is recorded.
- Worker start: `requires_isolated_workspace` reads the immutable profile mode
  from the role `bind_account` pinned instead of validating again; a primary
  user repository proved isolatable is not proved again per Worker (memo keyed
  by its `.git` device and inode); the input snapshot is one walk
  (`docs/modules/project_integration.md`).

Accepted for speed, to restore later: a birth in flight at a restart is lost
until replayed; durable refusals not found in attachment preflight (including
Stop races and root preparation failures) arrive through the await instead of
the call; the stored tool count trails by the reservations in flight; a source
edit or commit during the
snapshot walk can be partly in a Worker's input.

Alternatives rejected: a placeholder grant written by the call (a storage
write on the call path); a durable birth queue (a second dispatch state
machine); a synchronous birth with more parallelism (the Coordinator still
waits on each call).

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_births.py` (including
`test_parallel_delegations_are_born_concurrently`: four births held in root
preparation are all inside it at once, and every call has already returned)
and `test_the_coordinator_thread_makes_no_storage_call_to_delegate` in
`tests/storage/test_coordinator_delegation_reads.py`.

Measurement (owner report 2026-11-15, "delegating feels slow and blocks the
Coordinator"; Coordinator `coordinator-4950501b927a581b666a974cb9ce21b5`, four
delegations in one response, reproduce with `rg -a "9be6c286.*(early tool_start
emitted for delegate|stream_llm_response complete|delegate_project_work
completed)|Worker birth|worktree materialization" logs/app.log`):

| Phase | Where | Time |
|---|---|---|
| Model writes the response (four long briefs) | provider stream | 46 s (15:58:35 to 15:59:21); the calls' arguments completed at +16 s, +25 s, +32 s, +46 s |
| Call announced `queued` until the response ends | streaming executor | 30 s, 21 s, 14 s, 0 s |
| `delegate_project_work` handler, each of 4 | four `tool-parallel` threads, same second | 0.0 s (one later single call: 0.0 s) |
| Birth (validation, root, conversation, grant, Turn, dispatch) | own thread, concurrent | 0.5, 0.7, 0.7, 0.7 s |
| Worker worktree materialization (local `/tmp` disk) | Worker start, durable executor | 1.4 to 2.1 s |
| Coordinator's next model request | | 1 s after the response ended |

The tool neither blocks nor serializes: the wait the owner sees is the model
writing the later briefs, during which each finished call shows as `queued`
(`docs/contracts/EVENTS.md`), and a Worker starts only once the whole response
has ended. Decision: no change on the call path, which already returns before
any storage write. Starting a delegation while the response still streams
would shorten the first Worker's start by up to the remaining response time
(30 s here), but it is a side effect from a stream that may still be retried
or discarded: a discarded response would leave a running Worker the
Coordinator never asked for, and a retried one that reworded a brief under
the same key would be refused as `CommandReused`. The owner then asked for
the root cause to be fixed (2026-11-15): see the next section.

### A delegation starts when its arguments are complete

Problem (owner, 2026-11-15: delegating feels slow and blocks the
Coordinator): the streaming executor held every side-effecting call until the
model's whole response ended, so in the measurement above the first of four
delegations sat `queued` for 30 s after its own arguments were complete. The
tool itself takes about 0 s and births are concurrent; the hold was the whole
delay.

Decision:
- A tool may declare a stream-start port
  (`ToolSpec.stream_start_by_name`, `StreamStartPort` in
  `lib/tools/registry/_spec.py`). Declaring one asserts that the tool is
  idempotent by a key in its arguments and that its effect is owned by the
  issuing attempt. Registration refuses a port on a plugin or on a tool not
  declared a write that is idempotent (`idempotent_tools`) or names its key
  (`StreamStartPort.key_argument`, for a keyed write that keeps write
  reconciliation, so a dispatched Worker's unconfirmed call stays fenced).
  `delegate_project_work` and
  `send_project_message` (next section) declare one: `report_to_user`,
  `create_conversation`, `control_conversation`, `route_to_project` and
  `channel_send` carry keys too, but a human-visible report or channel
  message, a sidebar shell, a Session command or a forwarded owner Turn has
  no withdrawal.
- `StreamingToolAccumulator.on_tool_call_ready` starts such a call the moment
  its arguments are complete (the row is announced running, not `queued`),
  when the ordinary dispatch would run it without a human gate: the call is
  in the request's contract catalog and validates, the role policy admits it,
  it is no program child or dispatched Worker's call, and the conversation is
  not in attended Manual approval. Any other side-effecting call still waits
  for its final response. No timer, threshold or setting is involved.
- The start is `delegate(..., early=True)`: the same in-memory admission and
  the same deterministic key (origin attempt, `idempotency_key`), so the birth
  is the one the ordinary call would have started. `coordinator_births` marks
  it early.
- Adoption: the attempt's ordinary dispatch of the same call (same key, same
  arguments) adopts the early birth as it stands, in flight or finished, and
  never runs it twice; it returns exactly what an ordinary delegation returns.
  A replacement response that re-issues the same key and arguments adopts it
  too, so a provider retry or FloorRetry resend that reproduces the call
  keeps its Worker.
- Reconciliation (`StreamStartPort.settle` → `settle_streamed_delegations`)
  runs after every tool batch and when the attempt's model loop ends on any
  path (`_RootLoopHooks.execute_tools` and `_run.py`, both in `finally`).
  Every early birth no dispatch adopted by then (its response was discarded,
  its arguments changed, Stop or an error ended the loop, or admission refused
  the final call before its handler) is withdrawn: a born Worker at once
  through `ProjectDispatchService.withdraw_unadopted_birth` (the
  `cancel_project_work` revoke plus attempt cancellation, without its control
  check, because the birth that made the grant is the authority withdrawing
  it and the issuing attempt may have ended); a birth in flight cancels its
  Worker as it finishes, or never starts one. Waiters see that birth only
  after the cancel. Withdrawn births are not listed under `starting_workers`.
  A durable Stop withdraws the request's grants anyway (`_check_stops`), and a
  birth after Stop is refused by `source_for_task`.
- Replays stay idempotent: a regenerate (a new task of the same request)
  replays the same key and reuses the accepted Worker. A replay whose Worker
  was cancelled (by `cancel_project_work` or a withdrawal) is refused before
  any effect as `delegation_cancelled`, whose recovery names a new
  `idempotency_key`; a replay never revives a revoked grant.

Trade-off accepted: a discarded response whose replacement re-issues a key
with changed arguments gets `CommandReused` for that call (the first birth is
withdrawn), where before the first response had no effect; a regenerate
after a withdrawal gets `delegation_cancelled`. Both are visible refusals with
a recovery, and neither duplicates nor leaks a Worker.

Alternatives rejected: retiring early births on `on_provider_attempt_restart`
(the replacement response usually re-issues the same call, which would then
find a revoked grant); a delay or debounce before starting (a tuning knob,
AGENTS.md principle 5); starting every idempotent write early (report and
shell creation have no withdrawal, so a discarded response would leave an
effect the owner never saw); deriving the key from the call occurrence (a
regenerate would then create a duplicate Worker).

Measurement, time from a call's argument completion to its Worker birth
start. Before, from `logs/app.log` (early `tool_start` versus birth start =
completion minus its logged duration, one-second resolution): the four-call
response of 15:58:51/15:59:00/:07/:21 started its births at 15:59:21.3-21.5,
waits 30.4, 21.4, 14.4 and 0.4 s; a three-call response (16:03:34/:42/:51)
16.7, 8.7 and about 0 s; a two-call response (16:07:04/:05) 1.2 and 0.7 s.
After, in a reproduction that feeds the real `SSEAccumulator` four
delegations with the production gaps scaled by 1/10 (0.9, 0.7, 1.4 s): before
the change 2.83, 2.13, 0.73, 0.73 s (the response end plus the dispatch);
after it 0.004, 0.001, 0.000, 0.009 s. The fix is live only after the
execution host restarts.

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_stream_start.py` (a
scripted provider stream in which call 1's Worker birth is running before
call 2's arguments finish; discarded, Stop, stopped-before-start and
changed-reissue withdrawal; regenerate without a duplicate; tools without a
port and Manual approval held; the registration rule; settlement after every
tool batch).

### A project message starts when its arguments are complete

Problem (lead audit stage 2, S2-13, 2026-11-18): Global Router coordination
calls appeared to block for about a minute (`send_project_message` median
17.8 s, max 74.7 s in generation 97cc7708; `route_to_project` 58.7-63.2 s;
`channel_send` 76.1 s). The handlers did not wait: in every slow call
`logs/app.log` shows the early `tool_start` while the model streamed, then the
handler entered only at `stream_llm_response complete` and finished in 0.0-0.1 s,
the recipient's busy lane answering `[ProjectMessageWake] Conversation lane
busy; dispatch deferred` without waiting (Turn 8b2f60a3: early start 11:12:46,
handler 11:14:01, `completed in 0.0s`; Turn fc6d1295: 10:38:37 / 10:39:53;
Turn 7a5d8580: 08:41:18 / 08:42:21). The minute was the rest of the model's
response (more long messages, delegation briefs, board updates) during which
`StreamingToolAccumulator.on_tool_call_ready` held every side-effecting call
without a stream-start port (`lib/tasks_pkg/streaming_tool_executor.py`,
`_stream_start_port` returning None). The audit measured from the early
`tool_start`, so it counted that hold as call time.

Decision: `send_project_message` declares a stream-start port
(`_PROJECT_MESSAGE_STREAM_START` in `lib/tools/registry/_build.py`, keyed by
`idempotency_key`: one original per sender and key). It stays out of
`idempotent_tools`, so its effect class remains `reconcile` and a dispatched
Worker's unconfirmed send keeps its fence; a dispatched Worker never stream-starts.
- Start (`project_messages.start_streamed_send`): the ordinary send (admission,
  mailbox submit, `wake_idle_lead`) runs on its own thread the moment the
  call's arguments are complete, so a busy recipient lead takes the message at
  its next round and an idle one is woken, instead of after the sender's whole
  response. The same gates as a delegation apply (`_stream_start_port`).
- Dispatch-bound Coordinator Turns start too. Every lead continuation wake
  ("Continue the request…", restart continuations included) runs bound to its
  continuation grant, so `is_dispatch_task` holds for it. After the 13:55
  restart of generation 0b3b0163 the gate held every call of a dispatch task,
  so no lead send stream-started in the 9 continuation attempts (77 sends,
  GR `toolu_0151j3FajP71FwiMBuSyt4Gu` waited 331 s for its round), while the 2
  non-dispatch wake attempts started 5 of 5. Every one of those sends carried
  its required `idempotency_key` (the mailbox refuses a send without one). The
  audit read "no key" from the handler's `called with args=` preview, which is
  cut at 300 characters before a long message's trailing key. A dispatch task
  now starts only when its granted role is `coordinator` and `admit_call`,
  the dispatch admission its ordinary dispatch runs, admits the call; a refusal
  holds the call for that dispatch to report. A dispatched Worker (`project` or
  `files` grant) still never stream-starts.
- Adoption: the ordinary dispatch of a call with the same key joins that start
  and submits again, which returns the same original; a changed body is
  refused (`Idempotency key reused with changed message`).
- Withdrawal (`settle_streamed_sends`, on the same settlement points as
  delegations): a delivered message cannot be unsent, so a streamed message no
  dispatch adopted (its response was discarded, its body changed, Stop or an
  error ended the loop, or the final call was refused before its handler) is
  retracted by one `steer` message from the same sender naming the message ID
  (idempotency key `withdrawn:<sha256(key)>`, sent once).
- `route_to_project` (a forwarded owner Turn or steer) and `channel_send` (a
  message in the owner's chat) keep waiting for their final response: neither
  has a withdrawal, and a retraction in the owner's chat would be noise the
  owner sees. `consider_project_message` decides against a delivered revision
  and also waits.

Trade-off accepted: when a response is discarded after a message started, the
recipient lead may already have read it before the retraction arrives; it is
nonhuman context that carries no authority, and the retraction says so
explicitly. A replacement response that re-issues the same key and body
adopts the message (no retraction, no duplicate).

Alternatives rejected: committing early but hiding the message until adoption
(the recipient would see it no sooner, so nothing is gained); a new withdrawn
mailbox state (a contract and history-layout change for a correction an
ordinary message already expresses); starting `route_to_project` and
`channel_send` early (no withdrawal, see above); a delay or debounce (a tuning
knob, AGENTS.md principle 5).

Measurement, time from a send's argument completion to its durable mailbox
commit, with the recipient lead's lane busy (every wake answers `busy`). A
reproduction replays Turn 8b2f60a3's response through the real
`SSEAccumulator` and `StreamingToolAccumulator`: three sends whose arguments
completed at 11:12:46, :50 and :52, then its `update_task_progress` call, and
the response end at 11:14:01, with gaps scaled by 1/10, over the mailbox's
locked record client; the ordinary dispatch runs each call after the stream
ends. 10 runs, 30 sends each way: before the change (port disabled) median
7.10 s, p95 7.50 s, max 7.56 s (75 s at production scale, matching the
audit's 74.7 s); after it median 0.3 ms, p95 2.2 ms, max 12.5 ms. The fix is
live only after the execution host restarts.

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_message_stream_start.py` (the
first message is durable for a busy recipient while the next call still
streams; dispatch adoption without a duplicate; discarded and changed-reissue
retraction, sent once; the calls without a withdrawal stay held).

### Coordinator write tools read through dispatch contention

Problem: the same head CAS the reads race also serializes every Coordinator
write. At 16:15 CST on 2026-11-06, with about 17 Workers live,
`review_project_work` returned
`{"error": "Concurrent/uncertain dispatch update; read before retrying"}` and
the review was lost until the Coordinator replayed it by hand. The review's CAS
callback re-reads the Worker's full Turn projection on every attempt, so a
multi-megabyte result makes each of the bounded attempts slow enough to lose
again. `cancel_project_work`, `finish_project_request` and
`request_user_decision` share the same path.

Decision: `execute_coordinator_tool` runs these four commands through
`reread_on_contention`, exactly like delegation. The first attempt runs as
before; only contention enters the persistence wait, bounded by its existing
liveness contract (ACK, owner Stop, server shutdown). Each repeat re-validates
live authority and re-takes the publication lock, so a contended call never
holds that lock while it waits. Replay is unchanged: an identical review,
cancel, finish receipt or pending decision is a durable no-op (the decision
keeps its first due time), and a different one is still refused as immutable.
A real verdict met on the re-read — a revoked grant, a changed binding, an
already settled request — still refuses at once. `consider_project_message`
and `send_project_message` are unchanged: a send appends without any CAS and a
consideration CASes the recipient's own mailbox, never the dispatch head, so this
contention cannot reach them.

Alternatives rejected: waiting inside the publication lock (a contended review
would block publication), returning the contention to the caller with recovery
text (the Coordinator must then replay by hand, and the first attempt already
proved the command is idempotent), and shortening the review's evidence read
(useful, but a separate optimization, not an authority fix; it now reads Turn
metadata, see the compact receipts below).

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_contention.py`
(review/cancel/finish/decision races, replay, a revocation committed during
contention) on in-memory storage. Not covered: these tools racing the real
head on SQLite and PostgreSQL. There the head race is specified for a Worker's
writes only
(`tests/storage/test_project_dispatch_persistence.py::test_racing_head_commits_never_stop_a_segmented_worker`).

### Compact review and cancel receipts (2026-11-08)

Problem: `review_project_work` and `cancel_project_work` returned the whole
Coordinator view: the request (brief, granted tools, delegation policy) and the
Worker's complete Turn projection. In the 2026-11-08 Coordinator conversation
(first 145 of 315 messages) 51 reviews and 18 cancels returned 1.3 MB (review
median, max 12 MB) and 3.1 MB (cancel median, max 9.5 MB) of JSON; the model saw
a 12,000-character prefix, about 3.2-3.8k tokens of brief and tool names that
never reached the decision or delivery, or only "Tool returned a structured
result." when the JSON stayed under 1 MB. Each call also materialized the
projection and persisted it as a multi-megabyte artifact.

Decision: both tools return a compact effect receipt: `assignment_id`,
`conversation_id` (the Worker conversation; its title names the task in the
Coordinator's tool cards), `decision` (`accept`/`reject`/`rework`, or `cancelled`), `grant_revision`,
`revoked`, `result_revision`, `execution_status`, `checkpoint_sha`, `delivery`,
`canonical_delivery` and `next_step`, plus `acceptance_counts` for work with
acceptance checks (above). The service answers from the
settlement-only view, and the review's ownership pre-check and its revision
check read Turn metadata, so neither command materializes the projection.
Evidence stays with `get_project_assignment` and `get_conversation`. Measured on
two real calls of that conversation (receipt rebuilt from their recorded
delivery state, same token counter): an accepted review with a checkpoint went
from 3,469 to 365 visible tokens (its 7.2 MB result no longer read or stored), a
cancel from 3,473 to 155 (3.8 MB). `tests/project_mod/test_coordinator_receipts.py`
pins the shape and the metadata-only reads.

Alternatives rejected: keeping the full view and relying on the artifact readers
(every review still writes a multi-megabyte artifact and needs a second call to
reach its own outcome), trimming the view but keeping its evidence shape (the
request alone is 3-25k characters and is not an effect), and returning only an
acknowledgement (the Coordinator would need `get_project_assignment` again for
the revisions a rework or dependency pin copies and for the delivery state).

### A repeated Stop of an ended attempt does not withdraw later work

Problem: `abort_attempt` withdrew the whole conversation's dispatch even when
the attempt had already settled. A human Stop of an ended turn therefore planted
a NEW watermark dated at the retry, revoking every earlier grant in that
conversation — including grants created after the original Stop. The 11/06
`dispatch_grant_revoked` investigation of assignment 4143b9a2 did not come from
this path: that grant was revoked by its own Coordinator's
`cancel_project_work` at 07:49:59 CST (turn acd247ee, attempt 32ac97a5), so the
background-delivery verdict was correct. But the mechanism remains a real
authority hazard, and `control_session`'s stop branch already refuses it.

Decision: the withdrawal is dated at the attempt's settlement (`settledAt`,
falling back to `createdAt`, else epoch 0), never at the retry. The original
withdrawal is still retired — the watermark record keeps the maximum timestamp
and the revocation pass still runs — but it never moves forward to withdraw
grants created after the human's Stop. Live attempts are unchanged: their Stop
is dated now, before the executor is signalled.

Alternatives rejected: skipping the withdrawal entirely on a terminal retry
(would strand the original pending revocation, contradicting the recovery
contract that a repeated Stop retires it), and using the attempt's `createdAt`
only (a long-running attempt would misdate its own Stop).

Specifications: `tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_recovery.py`
(`test_stop_of_an_ended_attempt_never_withdraws_later_work`,
`test_terminal_attempt_stop_reuses_the_original_watermark`, and the existing
Stop-watermark suites).

### One dispatch head per owner and project (measurement and proposal)

Problem: every dispatch write commits against one CAS head per owner and
project (`ProjectDispatchRepository._change` committing through
`SegmentedDispatch.commit`), including every Worker's
model and tool records, reservations, delegations, bindings, reviews and
revocations. Since the contention fix (live 14:54 CST 2026-11-06) losers wait
instead of dying, so with about 20 Workers the head serializes the project.

Measurement (2026-11-06, an ad-hoc count of the `[AgentPersistence]` wait and
completion lines and the contention verdicts in `logs/app.log*`; the script is
not part of the repository): before the fix
there were 15 authority stops by contention and 16 'read before retrying'
verdicts. After the fix: 30 contended Worker model-occurrence calls (median
2.2 s, p90 8.2 s, max 70 s), 19 tool occurrences (median 2.9 s), 17 tool
reservations (median 1.8 s), and 10 Coordinator delegations whose waits
dominated: median 517 s, p90 608 s, max 739 s, about 72 minutes of Coordinator
wall time over 1 h 40 m. The delegation path re-reads a whole cohort per
attempt, so a busy head tears almost every snapshot; the Worker's own
single-assignment writes are two orders of magnitude shorter.

Proposal (report only; not implemented): keep one head for cross-assignment
invariants, but stop making every per-assignment ledger write depend on it.
Concretely, an occurrence/reservation commit for one assignment would commit
against that assignment's own document version (and, where a new document is
created, an assignment-scoped sequence) instead of the project head, while the
project head still fences multi-document reads, delegation, review, revocation
and migration. Invariants that must hold on every backend (TofuDB, SQLite,
Postgres): (a) a multi-document read's snapshot witness still covers every row
it reads, so per-assignment writes must advance some witness the reader checks;
(b) revocation and review still linearize with the assignment's own document,
so a contended per-assignment write can never resurrect a revoked grant;
(c) the head's `nextSequence`, counts and allowance indexes stay exact, which
is the part that forces either an assignment-scoped sequence or a head write
on document creation; (d) the SQL and native authorities implement the same
commit contract, so this is a storage-contract change, not only a repository
one. Because (c) and (d) span owners and both storage backends, this stays a
proposal until that contract is agreed; the contention fix above removes the
kills in the meantime.

### Dispatch authority stop causes

Problem: a dispatched task halts through the abort lane when its grant is revoked
or its authority can no longer be re-established (`validate_task`, the model,
tool and reservation observers, `admit_tool`, summary admission, or the
grant-withdrawal cancel port). Every such stop settled `interrupted/user_abort`,
so the Worker Turn, its activity row and `await_project_workers` presented it as
if the person had pressed Stop, and the Coordinator reported phantom Stops.

Decision: the site where the authority fails names the cause together with the
cooperative abort, before any further provider or tool call.
`admission.stop_on_authority_failure` stamps `_abort_reason`:
`dispatch_grant_revoked` when a `DispatchGrantRevoked` caused the failure (a
revoked grant, or one withdrawn by a durable Stop in the Coordinator or
recipient conversation), otherwise `dispatch_authority_lost` (lost carrier,
changed task identity, execution association or registered workspace, or an
unreadable grant record). `validate_task` applies it to every refusal, the
model usage observer to revocation races and unacknowledged usage writes,
`admit_tool` to authority it cannot re-establish (still raising
`ToolAdmissionRefused`), and summary admission to unknown continuation
authority. `abort_project_attempt` passes `dispatch_grant_revoked` to the exact
attempt cancellation: `TaskRuntime.abort_owned` stamps it under its lock only
when it is the task's first stop, and an attempt without a live executor
settles with it directly. Every stop also carries its provenance as
`_abortReasonDetail`, appended to the settled error detail: the cancel port's
caller names the actor (`cancel_project_work` names the Coordinator
conversation, task and assignment; the revoke API names the principal; a scope
change names its change), and an authority check names its refusal, which
includes the assignment and the Coordinator or Stopped conversation. A Worker's
failed Turn is therefore traceable to the Coordinator decision that ended it
without searching logs. The Turn records `failed`, `evidence:
external_authority`, the cause and a non-retryable envelope of the same kind
(`docs/contracts/TURN_SETTLEMENT.md`); the request activity row reports `failed`
with that `errorKind`. Whole-turn auto-retry never applies: the task is stopped
and `should_auto_retry_turn` refuses the envelope; the failed Turn keeps its
ordinary manual resume options. The first cause wins: a person's Stop keeps `interrupted/user_abort`, a task already
stopped is never restamped, and the port arriving after a Stop renames nothing.

Alternatives rejected: settling every abort-lane exit of a dispatch task as
failed (a person's Stop of a Worker would read as an authority failure);
reusing `tool_call_rejected` or `internal_fence_abort` (the first names one
call's refusal, the second a storage fence with a retryable envelope); returning
the refusal as a model-visible tool result (the Worker would continue past the
authority check); automatic retry (the same grant refuses again, so the recovery
is a new delegation). Remaining gap: a finite grant's exhausted model allowance
is a policy refusal, not lost authority; it still halts through the plain abort
lane and reads as a user Stop until it has its own typed cause. Specifications:
`tests/project_mod/test_project_dispatch_abort_cause.py`,
`tests/turn_lifecycle/test_dispatch_authority_settlement.py` and the dispatch
cases of `tests/turn_lifecycle/test_turn_verdict.py`.

### One live validation per admission
